| **Commands: the responder chain** — `CommandRoute`, `UiElement.AddCommandHandler`, `UiElement.CommandScope`, `ICommandResponder`, `ButtonBase.Command` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui, Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls, Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui | [45](plan/45-commands-and-focus-scope.md) staging steps **1, 3, 3b and 5**; step 2 is refuted (row below) and step 4 depends on it. `CommandRoute.Resolve` walks `Document.CommandFocus` → `Parent` → root → `Document.CommandResponder` → `Document.ApplicationCommandResponder`; the first responder wins and *its* `canExecute` is the only one asked; nobody responds ⇒ not executable (G3, G4). ⚠ **The chain continues past the element tree, which the doc's shape line promised and step 1 did not build.** AppKit's action chain runs on through the window, the document and `NSApp` to the app delegate, none of which are views, and a Vixen handler could only hang on a `UiElement` — so a view-model that wanted to own `edit.copy` had to own a piece of the view tree. `ICommandResponder` is one method, id → handler; `CommandResponder` is the table almost everything wants; `CommandHandler.Element` became nullable and gained `Responder`, because past the root there is no element and claiming one would make a diagnostic overlay lie. ⚠ **The level ships wired rather than empty**: `CommandRegistry` implements the interface over the table it already had and `EditorShell` installs it as its document's application responder, so a plain `Vixen.Ui` control bound to `edit.rename` resolves, greys and runs the editor's command through the registry's own gate and `Executed` — menus and toolbars are *not* bound, which is step 4 and still owed. Ordering asserted with a counter, not a comment: the further responder's lookups **and** predicates are both nought. Lifetime: the document holds the responders and never the reverse, `ICommandResponder` has no event or back-reference by design, and `UiDocument.Dispose` drops both slots and the `CommandsInvalidated` subscribers — proved against the collector by `A_long_lived_responder_does_not_keep_a_closed_document_alive`. ⚠ **Three parity claims checked and left alone**: `Focusable` + `TabIndex = -1` is already `acceptsFirstResponder` plus key-view-loop exclusion (`Focus.cs:206`), the dispatcher's single bubble-leg root handler already gives the inner control `performKeyEquivalent:`'s priority (`CommandDispatcher.cs:57`), and `IsCommandTransparent` is already "a menu is not in the responder chain". ⚠ **And one refuted**: `CommandRegistry` does *not* outlive its shell — `EditorShell.cs:102` is the only place one is made — so the `Changed` subscription is a cycle inside one ownership unit, not the ~95 MB-a-reload shape; unsubscribed anyway and asserted by subscriber count. `ButtonBase.Command` binds an id, so `Button`, `IconButton`, `MenuItem`, `ToggleButton` and `Link` all take one — `<MenuItem Label="Copy" Command="edit.copy" />` from `.vxml`, with `Disabled`, the label and the check state following the route and no rule written anywhere. `UiDocument.CommandsInvalidated` is one coalesced raise per `Tick` from three sources, and `ButtonBase` is its subscriber. ⚠ **`CommandFocus`, not `Focused`, is the origin, and that is what step 3 found:** `Menu.OnOpened` focuses its first item and `MenuBarItem` focuses on press, so a menu item resolving from `Focused` resolved from *inside the menu*, found nothing, and greyed every line — indistinguishable from a correct "nobody responds" until something looks. `UiElement.IsCommandTransparent` is a surface saying "I am not a place"; `Menu`, `MenuBar` and any bound control declare it. ⚠ **`Toolbar` was refuted, not built** — `ToolbarPresenter.Refresh` does exactly `Disabled` + `Caption` + `Checked`, all three of which the binding now does, so what is left is a `Panel` of `Button`s. 57 tests across five files |
| **Strings: the catalogue** — `StringId`, `StringCatalog`, `Strings`, `ControlStrings` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui, Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls, Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui | [46](plan/46-what-an-application-needs.md) § A3, all three parts. **Promoted out of `Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui/Localisation/`**, where an application that could not reference `Editor/` was left writing the literal. A `StringId` is an id and the source text at the declaration, so a missing catalogue shows English rather than `editor.menu.file`. ⚠ **The YAML did not travel**: `Save`/`Load` were the type's only use of `Vixen.Core.Yaml` and are now `Vixen.Editor.Ui.StringCatalogYaml`, because a catalogue promoted with a serialiser attached adds a package to the pin of every application that shows a word — sharpest for one publishing NativeAOT against a vendored closure. `Strings.Template` took a parameter for the same reason: it read `EditorStrings.All`, which stayed behind. ⚠ **The catalogue in use is a `Signal<StringCatalog>`**, so a language change re-labels a running interface with no code at any call site — every `@expr` is a region-scoped effect and reading a string makes it a consumer. Doc 46's acceptance criterion is `LocalisationTests`: a real `.vxml`, a catalogue changed between two frames, a bound label asserted to have changed; sabotaged by reverting the field, where both assertions read `"Close"` where they expect `"Zavřít"`. Public surface unchanged by the signal, which is the whole argument for doing it before the type shipped. ⚠ **The doc's twelve control literals are thirteen call sites and twelve distinct strings** — its table lists eleven sites and ten strings, and a sweep found `Navigation.cs:293` and `:314` (`"Previous page"`, `"Next page"`) that it missed. All thirteen go through `ControlStrings` now. ⚠ **What that does not buy**: a control assigns its labels in `OnCreated`, so it shows the language it was built in — the words are translatable, and a live control set that re-labelled itself would need an effect per label and somewhere to dispose it. ⚠ **The two dialog-button defaults doc 46 recorded as owed to itself are closed**: `DialogService`'s `"OK"`/`"Cancel"` were literals only because the catalogue was unreachable from a control assembly, and they are now `ControlStrings.DialogConfirm`/`DialogCancel` — fifteen declarations. ⚠ **The two sides are checked now rather than remembered**: `StringDeclarationAnalyzer` (VXS0310–0312) and `nuke CheckStrings` — see § 1.1 and § 1.11 |
| **Background tasks** — `BackgroundTask`, `BackgroundTaskManager`, `UiApplication.Tasks` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui, Platform/Vixen.Ui.Desktop, Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui | **Promoted out of `Vixen.Editor.Ui` (2026-08-25)** — application-framework machinery no application could reach — the fifth witness in [46](plan/46-what-an-application-needs.md), whose Part 3 listed it as the closest call it was *not* asking for ("would take it if it were offered"); offered and taken, and the row is now that document's *Offered, and taken* section. The *model* moved and the *task centre* stayed: `Tasks/BackgroundTask.cs` (209) + `Tasks/BackgroundTaskManager.cs` (199) are now `Core/Vixen.Ui`, and `Tasks/TaskCenter.vxml` (77) stays in the editor because it is made of `EditorStrings`, `ControlIcons` and the editor's own tags. ⚠ **The split cost one `@using Vixen.Ui` line** — the model referenced nothing editor-shaped, only `Vixen.Ui.Reactive`, so this is not the `StringCatalog` shape where two methods had to be left behind. Progress, status, title and state are signal-backed (shape unchanged: `float Progress { get; }` over a `Signal<float>`), `Tasks` is a `CollectionSignal`, and `IsCancellationRequested` is deliberately a *mirror* of the token because a signal read asserts the owning thread and the work polls from the pool. ⚠ **Reporting is safe from any thread, reading only from the UI one**: `Report` enqueues and `Pump` applies at one point in the frame, whose budget is a livelock guard rather than a nicety. ⚠ **The pump is wired, not offered**: `UiApplication` owns a manager and pumps it before raising `Frame`, pinned by `TheLoopPumpsTheTaskManager` running a real headless loop — an unpumped manager fails *silently*, at nought per cent. ⚠ **`BackgroundTaskManager` became `IDisposable`, and that is the leak fix**: an owner that dropped it left running work enqueueing into a queue nothing drains, and each closure pins the task, the manager and the assembly the delegate came from — the collectible `PluginLoadContext` shape this tree has paid for twice. `Dispose` cancels and then *stops accepting*; `EditorShell.Dispose` now disposes instead of `CancelAll`. 12 tests in `Core/Vixen.Ui.Tests/BackgroundTaskTests.cs` + 2 in `UiApplicationTests`, four sabotages confirmed |
| **The accessibility tree** — `UiElement.Role`/`AccessibleName`/`AccessibleDescription`/`AccessibleValue`/`AccessibleState`, relations, `UiDocument.AccessibilityInvalidated` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui, Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls, Core/Vixen.Ui.Testing | [46](plan/46-what-an-application-needs.md) § A2, and [09](plan/09-ui-framework.md) now has the § Accessibility it was said to have and did not. ⚠ **Greenfield, not an audit**: before this there were zero occurrences of the identifier `Role` in `Vixen.Ui`, `.Controls`, `.Controls.Advanced` or `Vixen.Platform`, three doc comments (`BuildContext.cs:620`, `Display.cs:161`, `Buttons.cs:31`), two of them in the future tense, and doc 09's single mention at its Testing table. Roles are **WAI-ARIA 1.2** tokens PascalCased and nothing else — which is why `img` is `AccessibleRole.Img`: the rule is that lowercasing a member name *is* the ARIA token for every member, so no exception table exists for the next role to be forgotten from. ⚠ **A control's accessible view is computed, never stored.** `NativeRole`, `NativeAccessibleName`, `NativeAccessibleValue` and `NativeAccessibleState` are virtual members answered from what the control already holds — no second copy, no callback, and no state in which a checkbox is ticked on screen and unticked to a screen reader. `ButtonBase`'s two overrides gave every button, menu item, tab, option and link in the assembly a role and a name in one edit. ⚠ **Three states are the framework's**: `Disabled`, `Focused` and `Focusable` are added for every element from `ElementState` and `Focusable`, so fifty controls cannot each forget one — and the symptom of a forgotten one, a screen reader announcing a greyed button as available, is invisible from inside the control. Cost per element is **one nullable reference (8 bytes) and no allocation** unless an application declares something on that element. Relations are the pairings no walk over `Parent` recovers: `TabItem` → its panel (`Controls`) and back (`LabelledBy`), `Select` → its root-parented popover (`Owns`) and → the chosen option (`ActiveDescendant`). ⚠ **`TextField.NativeAccessibleName` is `null` on purpose** — a placeholder is a hint, and it vanishes once there is a value, so a form named from placeholders loses its field names as it is filled in; an unlabelled field reports nothing and a gate fails it. `Panel`, `Card`, `Tabs` itself and every control part are `AccessibleRole.None`, so a bridge reads through them. `UiDocument.AccessibilityInvalidated` is `CommandsInvalidated`'s object one field over — flag set freely, read once from `Tick`, cleared before the handlers run — and ⚠ `Focus.cs` invalidates *outside* the command-transparency branch, the one place the two deliberately disagree. **Doc 09's promised ARIA-role snapshot is written**: `Vixen.Ui.Testing.AccessibilitySnapshot.Render` plus `Unnamed`, and ⚠ `Unnamed` is asserted **first** in every test because a snapshot matches an empty tree perfectly. Sabotage: dropping the role fails 4/10, dropping the name 5/10, dropping the `Owns` relation 2/10. **No platform bridge** — AT-SPI2, UIA and `NSAccessibility` are out of scope and are what the tree is for. ⚠ **Reconciled with § A3, which landed hours later on the same controls**: there are exactly two `NativeAccessibleName` overrides in both assemblies — `ButtonBase` (`=> Label`) and `TextField` (`=> null`) — and **neither hardcodes a literal**, so eleven of `ControlStrings`' thirteen call sites reach a screen reader through the catalogue with nothing written for them. The two that did not are the same shape and it is not drift: a translated word on screen that no element in the tree was reading — `PropertyGrid`'s filter box put its string in the `Placeholder` (deliberately not a name) and `ColorPicker`'s intensity caption had no `LabelledBy` from the slider beside it. Both announced nothing at all, and neither suite could see it because each asserts its own half. `AccessibilitySnapshot.Untranslated(root, ids)` is the gate, written **against the declarations rather than a list of controls** so that it keeps its meaning as the population continues; two reference windows, one per control assembly, each asserting `Unnamed` first. Sabotage: reverting `Dialogs.cs:91` to the literal `"Close"` names it, and so does reverting the property grid's name. ✅ **Both control assemblies are populated now** — **59 types override at least one of the four virtuals** and nine more establish a relation or assign a `Role` in `OnCreated`, and not one of them stores an accessibility field. Beyond roles it added the four live regions (`Alert`, `Toast` are `alert`, `ToastHost` is `log`, `EmptyState`/`Badge` are `status` — the things that appear where nobody was looking), two landmarks, four values, and relations for `Expander`→content, `KeyValueRow`→editor, `PropertyGrid` row→editor and `DockTab`↔`DockPanel`. ⚠ **`ComboBox` was not mechanical**: ARIA puts `role="combobox"` on the *text input*, so the outer element is `None` and the editor is a derived `TextBox` — a role *assignment* would have needed `aria-expanded` written into `DeclaredAccessibleState` from a handler, which is the second copy of the truth this design exists to not have. `TextBox` lost its `sealed` for it. ⚠ **`AccessibleDescription` was built and never fed**, and now has three feeders: `Tooltip.Attach` (the textbook `aria-describedby`, and the one with a consequence — a hover is a gesture a screen-reader user does not make), `Alert`/`EmptyState`, and `PropertyGrid`'s forty identical "Reset" buttons. ⚠ **Five *announced-only* strings** joined `ControlStrings` — a scroll bar, a hex field and a colour-space select have no caption at all, so their only words are the ones a screen reader says; `ScrollBar`'s reads the catalogue in a **virtual**, making it the one name in the set that follows a language change on a control already on screen. ⚠ **The gate is a reflection sweep over the assembly's type list**, not a window: every public `Control` with a parameterless constructor is built and held to "a tab stop must be in the accessibility tree", because a window is a list somebody has to remember to add to. It found three unnamed fields while it was being written. Sabotaged four ways. **Owed**: the pointer-only sub-parts of the canvases (`ColorField`, `GradientRail`, `NodeItem`, `TimelineTrack`, `ViewportGizmo`) have no role and should not get one before they get a keyboard; and a state a control *computes* — expanded, selected — does not raise `AccessibilityInvalidated`, which only structure and focus do. **No platform bridge**, as asked. ⚠ **And one thing the second reference window cost**: `Strings.Use` is a process-wide static and xunit runs test classes in parallel, so a window built under a pseudo-locale had its catalogue swapped out from under it — one test failing in a full run and passing on its own. Both assemblies have a `SharedCatalogue` collection with `DisableParallelization = true` now, repeated three times each side. 45 tests across five files |
| Editor context ⇒ `CommandScope` conversion (doc 45 steps 2 and 4) | ⬜ | — | ⚠ **Doc 45 § G2's premise is refuted and the doc has been amended.** The editor's context is *not* pushed from focus handlers: every push in the tree is a `PointerEvent` on the **capture** leg of a `DockPanel` (`EditorApplication.Contextual`, and four copies of the same eight lines in Blockout/Terrain/Water/Diagnostics), and its own doc comment says why — *"most of these panels do not take one… a viewport is not [focusable]"*. **Nothing under `Editor/` sets `Focusable = true`**, and the measurement says so: pressing in each of the seven panels that claim a context, **six leave `Document.Focused` null** and only `hierarchy` leaves anything (its `TreeView`'s row) — `CommandContextTests.Only_one_panel_in_seven_leaves_a_focus_behind_for_a_route_to_read`. Four of the nine contexts (`blockout`, `terrain`, `water`, `foliage`) are *modes*, which are not a place the focus can be at all. A focus route is right for `Vixen.Ui` and is the wrong derivation for this editor; what step 2 needs first is a decision about what the editor's scope is derived *from* |
| **A dialog that answers** — `DialogService`, `DialogSession<T>` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls | [46](plan/46-what-an-application-needs.md) § A4, **moved** out of `Vixen.Editor.Ui` rather than copied. `Dialog` already had modality — a real backdrop element, `IsFocusScope`, focus restored to whatever had it — and none of it answered a question; the 376 lines that did were in an assembly no application can reference. `ConfirmAsync` / `PromptAsync` / `ChooseAsync` / `ShowAsync<T>` hand back a `Task<T>`. ⚠ **The pump is `UiDocument.Ticked`, not a call the host makes** — same finding as `CommandsInvalidated`: `Update` returns early when nothing dirtied the document and asking a question dirties nothing, and `An_ask_dirties_nothing_which_is_why_the_pump_is_the_tick_and_not_the_pass` asserts `Update()` is false on the frame the ask is made. `EditorShell` no longer pumps it and disposes it instead of `CancelAll`ing. Four subtleties preserved and each sabotaged: deferred removal (8 of 15 fail — *this Dialog has been removed from its document*), completion from the pump (2), one at a time (3), `CancelAll` answers rather than drops (2); plus never subscribing (3) and never unsubscribing (1). 15 tests in `Vixen.Ui.Controls.Tests` |
| Pinch and rotate gestures | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui, Core/Vixen.Ui.Testing | `GestureRecognizer` tracks a second pointer and raises `TransformEvent` with cumulative *and* incremental scale and rotation, each past its own slop; `UiSubject.Pinch(scale, rotation)` drives it and `TransformTests` reads it. ⚠ A transform claims two pointers that had already begun drags, and cancels those drags rather than continuing them underneath |
| `Vixen.Ui.Composition` — `Component`, `@if`/`@switch`, keyed `@for` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui | |
| `scoped` scoping + a component stylesheet loaded once per **type** | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui | `StyleIsScoped` was parsed, carried, and then read by nothing |
| Named slot projection; LIS reorder pass | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Ui, Core/Vixen.Ui.Markup | Projection is built end to end — `<slot name=…>` binds to `BoundSlot`, the emitter calls `BuildContext.Slot`, `Component.Slots` resolves the default — but **no `.vxml` in the tree declares one**, so it is wired and unspent. The LIS pass does not exist and is correctness-neutral: a move that changes nothing returns immediately |
| `VirtualizingPanel` — the virtualisation primitive doc 09 asks for | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls | Realises on `LayoutFinished`. ⚠ Fixed row heights only — variable heights need a running-sum index, which is a different control. `TreeView` is migrated onto it |
| Image / texture draw command (`DrawContext.DrawImage`, `BatchKind.Image`) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui | Unblocked `Image`, `Viewport` drawing a `RenderTarget`, and the node-graph preview layer |
| Multi-window and DPI | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui, Platform/Vixen.Platform.Ui | `UiSurface` — one document, N windows, each with its own size, DPI scale, draw list and `vw`/`vh`. A window is a *surface* rather than a second document, which is what makes moving a panel between windows a `Reparent` and not a rebuild. `IUiWindowHost` is the seam `Vixen.Platform.Ui` fills |
| `Vixen.Ui.Markup` — VXML lexer/parser/binder/emitter, `#line` spans, incremental reparse | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Markup | 118 tests; byte-exact round trip over every *prefix* of a real file. `VxmlParser` takes the shared `Blender` and reuses green nodes |
| `bind:` update events | ⬜ | — | Two-way `bind:value` works; `bind:value:oninput` does not — the modifier list (`stop`/`prevent`/`capture`/`once`/`self`/`handled`) is `on:`-only, so which event commits the write is not selectable |
| `<self />` — a markup spelling for the component's own element | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Markup | **2026-08-24.** A `.vxml`'s markup roots are the host's *children*, so `on:keydown.capture` on the first root is a different element with different route coverage — which is why all five capture-leg handlers in the tree stayed hand-written in `OnComposed` after the `keydown` table entry landed. `<self />` emits `BuildContext.Host(this)` and applies its attributes to that; `VXML2015` refuses it anywhere but the top level, because inside an `@for` it would subscribe the host once per row |
| `on:….handled` — a handler that hears an event something else dealt with | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui, Core/Vixen.Ui.Markup | **2026-08-24.** The one modifier `BuildContext.On` could not apply itself: `stop`, `once` and `self` filter a handler it owns, while `handledEventsToo` is `UiElement.AddHandler`'s third argument. The subscription table's value type is now `Action<UiElement, Action<UiEvent>, EventSubscription>`, and entries call `EventSubscription.Listen` so that dropping the flag is not something that compiles |
| ⚠ `on:click` on a `Control` that raises no activation bound a handler nothing could raise | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls | **2026-08-24.** `ControlMarkup` has replaced the `click` entry since 2026-07-31 so a `<Button>` hears Space, Enter, an access key and `Activate()` — but it chose by `element is Control`, and only `ButtonBase` and `ColorSwatch` raise a `ClickEvent`. `<Card on:click>` and thirty other plain controls therefore bound nothing, silently. Both events are subscribed on every element now, and `Control.RaisesActivation` — asked of the element the tap landed on and everything up to the listener — keeps one press from counting twice |
| ⚠ `UiDocument.Cursor` had no consumer, so no `cursor-*` class changed anything | ✅ | Platform/Vixen.Platform.Ui | **2026-08-24.** The cascade resolved `cursor: pointer` correctly and nothing read it; doc 43 scored the family *works* because its probe called `CursorOf`. `PlatformCursor.Apply` maps it onto `IWindow.CursorShape` for the window the hovered surface is in, and both frame loops call it after `Update`. Every test is written against the window, because one written against the document would have passed throughout |
| `change:` — a value-change subscription markup can name | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Markup, Core/Vixen.Ui | **2026-08-22.** `on:change` could not exist: the `Subscribe` table holds `Action<UiElement, Action<UiEvent>, RoutingStrategy>` and no routed handler can carry a value. `change:Value` is not an event — it names a `[UiProperty]`, the same thing `bind:` names, and rides `UiElement.PropertyChanged`, so nothing is registered per control. ⚠ A change made while effects are draining is *not* reported: it came from a binding, and reporting it posts an undo entry for a value nobody touched |
| `refs` — a per-iteration handle | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Markup, Core/Vixen.Ui | **2026-08-22.** `ref` inside `@for` is still `VXML2010` and a `List<T>` would be worse — a surviving key's body is not re-run, so the list is a history of first appearances. `refs="@Faders"` files the element under the identity `BuildContext.For` reconciled on and drops it with the row's region; `VXML2013` refuses it outside a loop. Together with `change:` this took `AudioMixerView` from XL "never" to M |
| ⚠ A `[UiProperty]` was not registered until something read a static field of its type | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Generators | **2026-08-22.** The generated class had no static constructor, so it was `beforefieldinit` and the CLR could defer the `UiPropertyRegistry.Register` initialisers past the first instance. `TryFindFor` — which every `bind:` goes through and forces nothing — then reported that a freshly built `<Slider />` had no `Value`. Found while building `change:`; `bind:Value` on a control had the same defect, visible or not depending on what else the application had run. The generator now emits an empty static constructor, and `UiPropertyTests` asserts the type is not `beforefieldinit` (the existing test used `TryFind(Type,…)`, which forces the initialiser and so could not see it) |
| `Vixen.Ui.Markup.Generators` — `IIncrementalGenerator` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Markup.Generators | 19 tests, two of them real `dotnet build`s |
| `vixen` CLI path emitting generated C# to disk | 🟡 | Tools/Vixen.Cli, Tools/Vixen.Sdk | The `CoreCompile` hook carries something now: `VixenImport` runs `vixen import --addresses`, writes `Addresses.g.cs` and adds it to `Compile` *inside* the target, because the SDK's glob was evaluated before it ran. ⚠ It runs before the game assembly exists, so it is `--advisory` and is not the authority on content errors. It does not yet carry the VXML components or shader parameter keys doc [08](plan/08-asset-pipeline-and-addressables.md) names; the source generator covers VXML in-process instead |
| `Vixen.Ui.HotReload` — styles / markup / component replacement | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.HotReload | 27 tests |
| Hot reload driven against a **running window** | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.App, Editor/Vixen.Editor.Host | Doc 36 § P4. `EditorApplication` builds a `HotReloadHost` over the shell's document, registers it with `MetadataUpdate` (weakly, so a closed window is not a leak) and publishes it as a service, so a module's markup panel follows a `.vxml` edit once `dotnet watch` has recompiled it; `--hot-reload <dir>` adds a `HotReloadWatcher` over `.vcss`. ✅ **A watched sheet that is one the editor ships is replaced in place (2026-08-22)**: `HotReloadWatcher.Load` matches a file against the text of every sheet the document holds — the text is the only thing an embedded copy and its source share — and binds the path to that sheet instead of adding an `Author` copy over it, so a **deleted** rule stops applying. One match per theme, no per-theme plumbing. A file matching nothing is still an overlay, which is what a scratch directory is for. ✅ **`TaskCenter` is reloadable (2026-08-22)**: `EditorShell` builds the shell's one `.vxml` panel inside the constructor that makes the document the host is built over, so it could not have been tracked — `RemountTaskCenter` is the second step, taken by `EditorApplication` once the host exists. ✅ **`HotReloadHost.Replace` has a caller (2026-08-22)**: `MetadataUpdate.UpdateApplication` dropped the runtime's type list; passing it through lets a component that cannot rebuild *and* whose type the runtime just changed be recreated, which is the case where the update left the instance behind (a field added to a live type never runs its initialiser on an object that already exists). Only after a throw, only for a named type |
| `Vixen.Ui.Controls` — 40-odd standard controls, `ControlTheme` as `UserAgent` origin | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls | 259 tests over a real theme and font |
| `Vixen.Ui.Controls.Advanced` — Docking, TreeView, PropertyGrid, NodeCanvas, CodeEditor, DataGrid, Viewport, ColorPicker, CurveEditor, GradientEditor, Timeline | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls.Advanced | 313 tests |
| **Floating dock groups in real OS windows** | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Controls.Advanced, Platform/Vixen.Platform.Ui | A tab dragged off the window tears out; a drop inside docks. Windows keyed on the group object rather than its index so a rebuild does not blink them, drags tracked in desktop space so one can cross a boundary, and closing a window brings the panels home. Falls back to a rectangle inside the host where the platform has one window |
| `UiDocument` "layout finished" callback | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui | All six controls on it. `Control.WhenResized` gates on the box changing; `Update` refuses a nested call, which is what lets a `Refresh` that runs its own pass hang on the event |
| Undo inside controls | ⬜ | — | Deliberate rather than merely missing: `CodeBuffer` says undo belongs to the application, because it has to interleave with everything else the application did. The four `Changed` events are the seams a real stack subscribes to |
| `Canvas2D` | ⬜ | — | Doc [09](plan/09-ui-framework.md)'s P2, no editor consumer. `Samples/06-CanvasStress`, the intended proof, does not exist either |
| `OkLch.ToSrgb` real gamut mapping | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Core.Mathematics | `GamutMap` does CSS Color 4's binary search on chroma in OkLch, holding hue where a per-channel clamp shifted it. `StyleValueParser` brings wide-gamut authored colours in; `UiGeometryBuilder` maps every emitted colour into the surface's gamut. `GamutMapTests` + `UiGamutTests` |
| `StyleTree.AppendChild` O(children) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Styling | Amortised constant now: a child run reserves capacity beyond its count and doubles on overflow, so copies fall off geometrically. ⚠ The reserved slots hold stale ids and nothing may read them — every reader is bounded by `ChildCount`, not by the arena's length |
| `Vixen.Ui.Testing` harness + software rasteriser | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Testing | Selector queries, interactions (taps, drags, `Pinch`), box and visual assertions. Group opacity, a third finger and the remaining box assertions are owed — see the README's *Owed* |
| `Samples/02-HelloUi` | ✅ | Samples/02-HelloUi | 8 001 elements at 0.436 ms, 0 B — exit criterion met with margin. Rewritten in `.vxml`/`.vcss` on `Vixen.Ui.Desktop` (2026-08-23); the hand-rolled 500-line host is gone |

## 1.8 Raven and shaders

| Feature | Status | Where | Blocked by / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hand-written lexer + recursive-descent parser (Phase 5b migration) | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven | ANTLR `.g4` kept as a differential oracle |
| Green/red tree from `Syntax.xml` (79 + 13 node types), trivia, spans | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven | |
| Semantic phase, target-independent IR | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven | |
| GLSL + SPIR-V emitters, `spirv-val` on every module | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven | Golden `spirv-dis` snapshots |
| Incremental reparse with green-node reuse | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Core.Syntax | |
| `protocol`, shader inheritance, compile-time generics, `compose` | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven | |
| Atomics (8 on `int`/`uint`) | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven | Landed for the VFX compaction path |
| `.rvnlib` / `.rvnfx` artefact formats | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven | |
| `Raven/Library` — Core, Shading, Geometry, Material, Pipeline, PostFx, Ui, Vfx | ✅ | Raven/Library | Every shader reaches both backends under `glslc` and `spirv-val` |
| **Bindless texture arrays** (`Texture2D[]`) | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven · Raven/Library | The only unsized array outside a storage block, and the one that is descriptors rather than memory: `OpTypeRuntimeArray` with no stride under `RuntimeDescriptorArray`, `uniform texture2D t[]` under `GL_EXT_nonuniform_qualifier`, `Count == 0` in the reflection. Spent by `MaterialSurface.rvn` and `Terrain.rvn`, reached from the host by `WorldRenderer` and `EffectLoader`. ⚠ Every subscript is decorated `NonUniform` on **both** the index and the pointer — one without the other validates and then reads one descriptor for a whole subgroup |
| **String interpolation** | ⬜ | — | Needs lexer modes; nothing shipped uses it |
| **Workgroup-shared memory** | ✅ | Raven/Vixen.Raven · Raven/Library | `groupshared` is a parser modifier, a flag on `SourceFieldSymbol`, and a per-entry-point reachability pass in the `Lowerer` — ⚠ a stage declares only the shared variables its own reachable code touches, because workgroup memory is a budget rather than a namespace. Emits as an `OpVariable` in SPIR-V's `Workgroup` class and a GLSL `shared`, with two barrier intrinsics carrying `AcquireRelease` over `WorkgroupMemory`. Held by `GroupSharedTests`, and **spent**: `Culling.rvn`'s traversal queue, `VisibilityTiles.rvn` and `WaterTiles.rvn` need it. **Nothing is blocked on this**, GPU sorting included |
| `Vixen.Raven.Transpile` (SPIRV-Cross → ESSL/HLSL/MSL/WGSL) | ⬜ | — | ADR-012 says SPIRV-Cross owns these targets. **No SPIRV-Cross package in `Directory.Packages.props`**, and no such project under `Raven/` |
| Cross-compilation test pass | ⬜ | — | Blocked on the row above |
| Nuke `CompileShaderLibrary`; SPDX enforcement in `CheckFormat` | 🟡 | build/Build.Shaders.cs · build/Build.cs | `CheckShaders` is the half that matters and exists: it builds `Vixen.Raven.Cli`, recompiles each editor library shader from its whole import closure, and fails if a committed `.spv` has drifted. ⚠ A shader's import closure is one compilation (`raven compile --source`) — the alternative is a chain of `.rvnlib` in dependency order, a second build graph that has to agree with the `import` lines. **SPDX enforcement now runs first in `CheckFormat`** — see § 1.15's row for its scope |
| Numeric BRDF gate (GPU compute readback vs. C# port) | ✅ | Platform/Vixen.Raven.Gpu.Tests | The shipped `Brdf.rvn` — not a copy — evaluated on a device over 256 (angle, roughness) samples against arithmetic *derived* from Walter 2007 and Schlick 1994, because an oracle sharing an implementation with its subject is not one. Plus a **white-furnace** integral needing no reference: directional albedo ≤ 1, monotonically decreasing, 0.885 → 0.438, with a lower bound asserted beside the upper one. ⚠ `ClampAlpha`'s 0.002 floor is part of what the BRDF *is* — a reference without it measures a different function below 0.045 roughness |
| Per-backend layout gate (reflection offsets vs. GPU readback) | ✅ | Platform/Vixen.Raven.Gpu.Tests | The host writes bytes at the offsets the reflection reports and the shader reads members by name, so a member the two disagree about arrives holding its neighbour's value. Nothing asserts what the offsets *should* be — that is std140's business, and a hard-coded table would be a second implementation of the rules. ⚠ The block's hazards are asserted *present*, because a gate is only a gate for what it can observe: `beta@28` packs into `direction`'s tail, the array's stride is 16 and the matrix's is 16 |
| Negative-diagnostic fixture pairs | 🟡 | Raven/Vixen.Raven.Tests | 127 diagnostic ids against 34 `AssertNoDiagnostics` sites: most ids have a trigger, few have the negative that proves the rule does not over-fire |
| Stream interpolation control; per-module flat IR namespace | ⬜ | — | Recorded in doc [07](plan/07-raven-shader-pipeline.md) §Streams and §D. `flat` is applied automatically where an integer varying requires it (`StageInterface.MustBeFlat`), but an author cannot ask for `noperspective` or decline interpolation; IR struct and function names stay one flat namespace, collision-resolved in the `Lowerer` |
| `Vixen.Shaders` — typed parameter/permutation keys, std140 writers | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Shaders | Generated from Raven reflection |
| Effect system + 3 cache tiers (`EffectStore`, disk, remote) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Shaders | |
| `Tools/Vixen.ShaderCompiler` (`PermutationClosure`, `EffectBundleBuilder`) | ✅ | Tools/Vixen.ShaderCompiler | Zero-runtime-compilation criterion asserted by test |
| `Tools/Vixen.ShaderCompilerService` | ✅ | Tools/Vixen.ShaderCompilerService | |
## 1.9 Rendering, video and XR

| Feature | Status | Where | Blocked by / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RenderSystem`, `RenderObject`/`RenderNode`, features, views, stages, sort modes | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | |
| `VisibilityGroup` (job-parallel) + `GpuVisibilityGroup` (Hi-Z, indirect args) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | Falls back where it cannot run |
| Mesh, transform, skinning, instancing, material, lighting, shadow-caster features | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | |
| **Two-phase occlusion culling** (`GpuVisibilityGroup.TwoPhase`, `Culling.rvn`'s `Late` permutation) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | Removes the frame of staleness. ⚠ The late pass writes a **difference**, not an answer — the union would draw every visible object twice. Needs the readback off; `LatePhaseRan` says which happened |
| **Sprites, sprite sheets, 9-slice** | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering · Core/Vixen.Ui · Core/Vixen.Core.Mathematics | `SpriteRenderFeature` + `SpriteGeometry` on the renderer side, `DrawContext.DrawNineSlice` on the interface side, sharing `NineSlice` because the two assemblies cannot reference each other. A nine-sliced image is the same `Image` command and the same batch as a stretched one. ⚠ Tiling is the renderer's only: a repeat count needs the texture's pixel size, which a draw list does not have |
| Compacted draws | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | `GpuDrawArguments.Compact` — one command per batch, count read from a buffer the host never sees. A clustered `ForwardPlus` frame merges; ⚠ the uniform-light-list path binds a block per object at a dynamic offset and cannot |
| `GraphicsCompositor` as an asset, resolvable by address | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | Asserted in `Vixen.Assets.Tests` |
| **`WorldRenderer` in the boot path** | ✅ | Tools/Vixen.App | `AppGraphics` is the host's half — device (`GraphicsHost`: Vulkan where there is a surface, Null where there is not), swapchain, `EffectSystem` fed from the content build's `shaders.effects`, the compositor (a project's by address, or a built-in one-pass frame), and the per-frame order: lend the image under a name, draw the world, hand the open list to `Game.OnRender`, submit, present. `--vixen-frames N` therefore exercises the whole renderer on a machine with no GPU |
| **Camera component → `RenderView`** | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | `CameraExtractionSystem`: lowest `Order` wins, `PreRender` after the transforms. ⚠ A world with no camera leaves the view alone and says so — a zeroed matrix is a black frame that reads as a broken renderer |
| Materials — composable feature tree, 2 workflows, 7 shading models, both layering forms | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | Every combination through `glslc` + `spirv-val` |
| **A material feature that samples** (`TexturedMetalRoughnessSurface`) | ✅ | Raven/Library · Core/Vixen.Rendering | Needed four things that did not exist: `Texture2D[]` as a type, `[Shared]` so every sampling feature names one table, `uv` on `MaterialData`, and a table slot arriving as a value. ⚠ The authored record carries a *name* and no handle, so a material still serialises on a machine with no device |
| Transmission / refraction | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Rendering.Water · `Raven/Library/Water` | [35](plan/35-water.md) § D8 closed the *pass*: `!Water` reads a `!Copy` of the scene colour plus depth and integrates absorption and scattering over the path behind the surface, with `SingleLayerWaterShading` as the lobe beside it. ⚠ **What is closed is the pass, not the general case** — the volume integration is homogeneous and the geometry is a surface with something behind it, so water and thick tinted glass are answered; a refracted *ray* through a solid with two interfaces is not, and `Transmission.Refract`/`ScreenOffset` remain the thin-surface approximation. Owed: the general transmissive material, which takes the same seam — a copy, and a pass that reads it |
| Bindless material textures (a feature that samples needs a binding index) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | `MaterialRenderFeature.Textures` + `TextureIndices`: the shader declares a `uint`, the texture takes a table slot, and the slot goes into the material's own block. ⚠ Registered per material and **idempotently** — this runs every frame, and a table asked for the same view sixty times a second raises a count nothing lowers |
| Lighting — directional/point/spot/tube/rect, clustered binning, IBL, reflection probes | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | `EnvironmentBaker` + `SphericalHarmonics` on the CPU. ⚠ Intensities are **photometric**: `LightComponents.Default` gives a new directional light 100 000 lux (daylight), not 1 — see [physical lighting](guide/rendering/physical-lighting.md). A pass lit by an authored 0–1 tint in a cd/m² frame is pixel-identical to a pass that never ran |
| **Light probes** (tetrahedral interpolation) | ✂️ | Core/Vixen.Core.Mathematics · Core/Vixen.Rendering | **Withdrawn as an approach, not deleted as code.** `ExactPredicates` + `DelaunayTetrahedralization` + `LightProbeVolume` are built and correct, and nothing outside their own tests constructs them; the GPU half will not be built, because [19](plan/19-lighting-and-global-illumination.md) § 4 retires tetrahedral interpolation in favour of § L2's brick-and-indirection field, which places probes on a lattice and needs no predicates and no triangulation to sample. The predicates stay because other geometry wants them |
| **Distance fields** (bake, clipmap, tracer, traced pass) | ✅ | `Vixen.Rendering.DistanceFields` | [19](plan/19-lighting-and-global-illumination.md) § L1. Exact bake with a voted sign, camera-snapped scrolling clipmap, CPU tracer, volume textures, compositor node, Raven module, `DistanceFieldAo` — and `DistanceFieldAoImageTests` draws with it on a device |
| **Irradiance field** (bricks, indirection, L1 payload, leak mitigation) | 🟡 | `Vixen.Rendering.IrradianceFields` · Core/Vixen.Rendering | [19](plan/19-lighting-and-global-illumination.md) § L2, and see the [README](../Core/Vixen.Rendering.IrradianceFields/README.md). 4³ probes in a 5³ footprint with refinement, borders, dilation and both biases (§ G3's four leak mitigations); a CPU reference filler and a compute filler that agree probe by probe on a device; `IndirectDiffuse` draws the result and `ForwardPlus` composes it into its ambient term. Filler B renders its own cubes and shades from the field, so pass *n+1* bounces off pass *n*; coarsening is `Split`'s exact inverse with hysteresis. Owed, no known defects: `Deferred` (blocked on the Phase 10 pass), the bake-time step that would size a baked field, and two unmeasured optimisations (narrowing the repair to dirty bricks, batching filler B's one-submit-per-probe) |
| **Screen probe gather** (octahedral map, lattice, reference, traced atlas) | 🟡 | `Vixen.Rendering.ScreenProbes` · Core/Vixen.Rendering | [19](plan/19-lighting-and-global-illumination.md) § L3, and see the [README](../Core/Vixen.Rendering.ScreenProbes/README.md) for every claim below. The whole chain runs on both processors and the two agree: octahedral map with exact per-texel solid angles, 16-px lattice with per-probe validity, placement from the frame's own depth and normals, trace (screen march first, then the irradiance field), resolve to L1, temporal accumulation, spatial filter and bilateral upsample — scheduled by `ScreenProbeGatherRenderer` as one node. § L3's exit criteria are asserted in `ExitCriteriaTests` against a 4096-sample Hammersley reference (1% unshadowed, 5% RMS / 10% worst under occlusion), with a camera cut and a fast pan as the temporal cases; a resize is a deliberate `Reset`. `ScreenTraces` is off by default — probe origins are a frame older than the depth the rays march. Owed, quality rather than criteria: the adaptive probes' device half, importance sampling, bilinear history taps |
| **Surface cache** (cards, atlas, radiosity) | ✅ | `Vixen.Rendering.SurfaceCache` | [19](plan/19-lighting-and-global-illumination.md) § L4. Cards fitted by dominant-axis vote, shelf atlas, traced capture, direct sun behind a shadow ray, double-buffered radiosity — the Cornell box converges against a five-bounce Halton path tracer within a stated 5% (measured 1.2). `SurfaceCardIndex` narrows sampling, `SurfaceCacheLight`/`SurfaceCacheGather` match the CPU pair at zero drift, `ISurfaceCacheSource` makes "what a hit sees" a compose slot so the screen probes inherit multi-bounce light, and `SurfaceCacheRenderer` keeps it live (capture one card a frame, one frame late, because a readback follows its submit). One-pass MRT capture landed. Nothing in § L4 is owed |
| **Traced reflections** (mirror, rough, fallback) | ✅ | `Vixen.Rendering.Reflections` | [19](plan/19-lighting-and-global-illumination.md) § L5, and see the [README](../Core/Vixen.Rendering.Reflections/README.md). The layer that reuses everything below it: a mirror ray marches L1's tracer, a hit answers through L4's `SurfaceCacheRadiance`, a miss asks `IReflectionFallback` — the seat doc 06's probes take through `ReflectionProbeMissSource`, which retires the "blended against the sky" caveat. Rough surfaces above a threshold read L2's field instead of tracing, with a blend band closed-formed on both processors; the screen answers first, through the shared HZB march. `ReflectionTrace` is the kernel and `ReflectionRenderer` the node, held against the CPU reference within a stated hundredth (measured four thousandths, all of it the field read's hardware trilinear). Nothing in § L5 is owed |
| **Ray tracing** (RHI, query, tracer, referee) | ✅ | `Vixen.Rendering.RayTracing` · Core/Vixen.Graphics | [19](plan/19-lighting-and-global-illumination.md) § L6. A median-split triangle BVH as the reference — deterministic builds, front-to-back traversal, two-sided Möller–Trumbore, held against brute force at four-times-fewer visits. The RHI concept is `HasRayTracing` (build/refit + queries + buffer device addresses, all three or none), the two-level build, the acceleration-structure descriptor kind and the 64-byte shared instance record; Vulkan 1.2+ implements it, everything else refuses honestly, the Null device answers coherently. Raven gained the query as one intrinsic — `AccelerationStructure.Trace` expands to the whole query sequence in both emitters — and `RayQueryField` puts it behind L1's interface so every kernel composes it unchanged. ⚠ The golden comparison skips on MoltenVK, which exposes neither extension — stated, not discovered. Owed, quality: the hit's true normal off the vertex buffer, SAH, refit |

The reasoning is in `AmbientCombineRenderer`'s own remarks. | Per-object reflection probe selection | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering · `Raven/Library/Pipeline` | Built and wired. `ForwardLightingRenderFeature` picks a probe per object and writes `probeIndex`/`probeWeight` into an object record; `ClusteredShading.ProbeIndex` reads it back through `SV_InstanceID`; `SceneLighting` fills the array in the selector's own order. ⚠ **`UseObjectRecords` requires `UseTransformRecords`** — what addresses a record is the instance index, and it only holds the object's slot because the transform path put it there; `CompositorBuilder` enables it only where transforms turned on, in a deliberate second pass. ⚠ `ProbeCount` is a compile-time array size (default 4): a scene with more probes binds the first N and `SceneLighting.Dropped` counts the rest, which reflect the wrong room rather than nothing. Raising that ceiling is what [23](plan/23-bindless-materials.md) would buy |
| Shadows — CSM, cube, spot, atlas, static caching, PCF/PCSS | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | The cascades' static cache reaches a document since 2026-08-21 and did not before: the node was complete and only a test ever set `StaticCasterStage`. Three halves have to agree — `staticStage:` and `slack:` on a `!ShadowMap`, `GraphicsOptions.StaticCasterStages` naming the stages a static entity is extracted into *instead of* the ordinary caster ones, and `StaticShadowCaster` on the scene's own geometry. The cache atlas is the node's texture rather than a declared one, because every `!Resource` is transient; the working atlas needs `CopyDestination`. Redrawn only when a cascade re-fits or `StaticVersion` moves, so `slack: 0` is a cache that costs a 64 MB copy a frame and saves nothing — reported through `Degraded`. Measured on Samples/13 under `VIXEN_WALK`, 256 frames: 1 624.5 → 1 407.0 draws recorded a frame, 24 rebuilds of 256, same picture to 0.0001/255 at an identical pose. ⚠ Two defects the split uncovered, neither the cache's: a shader variant keyed without the stage that imposed it, so the first of two caster stages decided which `RenderStage.Parameters` every stage drew with and an unfilled one drew with set 2 empty — a segfault in the submit where there is no validation layer; and a `!VirtualShadow` node given only half the casters, which outranks the cascades wherever it has a drawn page and so removes the level's sun shadow from most of the screen while every counter reads healthy |
| **Virtual shadow maps** — clipmap, page marking, page residency, per-page draws, composed lookup | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Rendering · `Raven/Library/VirtualShadows` | [22](plan/22-virtualized-geometry.md) phase 7. A directional clipmap and a map per spot: the level is chosen **per pixel** from that pixel's own world footprint, only marked pages are allocated, only newly allocated or invalidated ones are drawn. ⚠ The snap is to a whole **page**, not a texel — that is what makes a page's world footprint bit-identical between frames and therefore cacheable at all, and a page is addressed **toroidally** (`VirtualShadowMap.ToroidalOf` against `VirtualShadowLevel.Origin`) so that identity survives the window sliding: a camera walking one page retires the thirty-two pages of the column that wrapped rather than the level's thousand and twenty-four. Measured on Samples/13 under `VIXEN_WALK`: pages absent at the table upload 10.45 → 2.12 a frame, invalidations 49.4 → 2.3, blinking frames 18.1 % → 2.9 %, with the refit *cadence* unchanged at 0.340 levels a frame — what changed is what a refit costs. A page enters the table when it is *drawn*, not when it is allocated, and the lookup answers a *sample* (a visibility and whether it knew), so `ClusteredShading.Shadow` falls through to the cascades where the map has nothing. Improvement 6's second consumer: `PageResidency` serves it unchanged. ✅ **Per-caster invalidation landed 2026-08-25** (task #250), and it was the correctness item of the three: a page is kept until something says its depths are stale, and only the light and the levels ever said so — a moved *object* said nothing, so its shadow stayed where the object had been for as long as the level did not also move. ⚠ This row used to excuse that with "the page it is in keeps being re-marked", which was false in the other direction too: `VirtualShadowPages.Owe` deliberately refuses a page that is already published, so a re-mark did nothing for one. `Collect` now walks the `RenderObjectStore` after the fit and retires **both** footprints of anything whose bounds — or world matrix, where `CasterTransforms` names one, because a sphere is invariant under rotation and a shadow is not — differ from last frame's; the end it *left* is the visible one, since retiring only where it now is leaves the stale silhouette in the air beside a correct one. The page set is `VirtualShadowMap.PageSpan`, whose *rectangle* is the right one rather than a guess — a shadow map's projection looks along the light, so a caster's footprint in that clip space is where its shadow lands, with no "how far along" term to get wrong; only the bound inside it is conservative (the sphere's box rather than the sphere). It clamps where `PageOf` refuses — a volume over the edge still shadows the part inside. `MovedCasters` / `CasterInvalidations` separate the scene's cost from the clipmap's. Measured on Samples/13 over 900 walked frames (headless *with* `--vixen-capture`, M1 Max): 8.06 movers a frame retire 4.17 published pages, draws 0.72 → 4.87, absent 0.94 → 4.13 of an unchanged 55.86 demanded, with `refit` (0.4433), `resident` and `allocations` identical and 832 of 900 frames agreeing on all three — so coverage goes 98.3 % → 92.6 % and the 3.2 pages a frame that stopped being answered were being answered with the shadow of an object that had walked away. The budget saturates on 35 of 900 frames, so what is left is the same structural one-frame lag a refit pays. ⚠ The `CasterInvalidations` counter read 40.9 in its first draft because `Invalidate` answers "there was an allocation" again for a page an earlier caster of the same frame already retired; it counts published pages *lost*. **Owed:** clusters do not cast through it — `Culling.Accept` appends every view's cut to one buffer behind three global counters, so an entry carries no view tag and per-page culling needs a list per view (a change to phase 3's output, touching the two rasters, the tiles and the resolve); virtualized meshes cast through phase 1's fallback mesh meanwhile. Also owed: point lights, which are six maps rather than one and want a cube address space |
| Punctual shadow caching | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering | `PunctualShadowRenderer.Cached`, and `cached: true` on a `!PunctualShadows` node. A lamp keeps the slot it was drawn into and the texels in it; a tile is redrawn only when the lamp's own projection inputs changed bit-for-bit, when the casters whose bounds meet *that tile's* frustum changed — ids, bounds and world matrices — or when the host bumps `CasterVersion` for something bounds and transforms cannot show. **Not `ShadowMapRenderer.StaticAtlas`'s shape, deliberately:** a cascade is fitted to the camera, so only its static half is cacheable and putting the halves back together costs a whole-atlas copy every frame; a punctual frustum does not know the camera exists, so the whole tile is cacheable and there is no second stage and no copy. One render pass per redrawn tile, because a `LoadAction.Clear` is confined by the pass's **render area** and one pass over the atlas would wipe every kept tile — `VirtualShadowRenderer` learned that the expensive way. ⚠ **The trap that made it worth nothing until it was measured:** a frame that culls on the device *without a readback* hands the host the conservative set, so each of sample 13's 108 tiles has the whole level in its CPU work list and a cache that hashed that list redrew all 108 whenever anything anywhere moved — with every counter reporting success. The node re-tests each entry against the tile's own frustum, and `TilesMoved`/`TilesDisturbed` is what tells the two failures apart. Measured on Samples/13 under `VIXEN_WALK`: 8 306 → 1 297 draws recorded a frame, 4–8 tiles redrawn of 108; standing still, 7 647 → 436. The atlas is 256² tiles of boxes, so its GPU scope was already at the timer's noise floor — what this removes is command recording |

| **Blend shapes** — sparse quantised deltas, the import, the kernel and the compute scatter | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Rendering · `Raven/Library/Pipeline` · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Assets | [33](plan/33-character-creator.md) § D4's design, built as a vertical slice and no longer ⬜. `MorphTargetData` is the storage — `(index, Δposition, Δnormal)` per moved vertex, 16-bit snorm against a **per-target** range, sorted by index; sixteen bytes an entry with normals and ten without, so a head with twenty shapes touching four thousand vertices each is 1.28 MB, resident and shared by every instance. `MorphKernel` is the arithmetic written once and `Pipeline/MorphScatter.rvn` is its transliteration, held together on a device by `MorphScatterDeviceTests` — exactly where a weight of one puts a vertex, and to a measured 10⁻⁶ under awkward weights, which is what a fused multiply-add is worth. Import is real and asserted against Assimp: `ModelReader` reads `aiMesh.mAnimMeshes` for both glTF and FBX behind `importBlendShapes`, and ⚠ **an `aiAnimMesh` is a whole replacement vertex array rather than a delta** — the subtraction is ours, it is against the *scaled* positions, and getting either wrong renders plausibly. ⚠ **One dispatch per active target with a barrier between, which is § D4's sketch amended**: two shapes may move the same vertex — that is what a corrective is — and Raven has no float atomic, so one dispatch over the concatenation would resolve to whichever invocation landed last. ⚠ **Nothing renormalises the morphed normal**, deliberately: a shape may cancel one exactly, `Vector3.Normalize` gives up below an absolute 10⁻⁶, and `ForwardPlus` already `SafeNormalize`s the interpolated normal at 10⁻⁴. **Owed:** the render feature — nothing yet allocates the per-instance buffer, copies the base in, dispatches and points `MeshDraw.VertexBuffer` at it, though that seam is the right one and is per render object already; a scalar weight track on `AnimationClipData`, which is a format change; and the cluster-page path, which packs its own vertex records. [Blend shapes](guide/rendering/blend-shapes.md) |

It is `VK_KHR_depth_stencil_resolve`, which the engine already enabled as a dynamic-rendering prerequisite, plus a `DepthResolveMode` on the depth attachment. ⚠ The mode has to be named because averaging depth is meaningless, and the default is `Max` — **the sample nearest the camera, under reversed-Z**. ⚠ **And naming it is not enough: the device has to advertise it.** Vulkan requires only `SAMPLE_ZERO` of an implementation, and lavapipe — the driver every golden image in CI runs on — offers exactly that while rendering 4× depth attachments happily, so `DepthResolveImageTests` was submitting invalid usage there from the day it landed. `GraphicsDeviceFeatures.SupportedDepthResolveModes` now carries the answer and the Vulkan backend clamps to `SampleZero` rather than submitting a mode the device declined |

Wakes and splashes are one `WaterDisturbance` event with two consumers and ⚠ **draining does not empty it**. *Owed*: `glBindImageTexture` on the GL backend, and the `Vixen.Vfx` spray the buoyancy solver already produces the events for |
| **The water toolset** — one mode, three verbs, the zone panel's derived numbers, and the wiring | 🟡 | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Water · Core/Vixen.App.Hosting · Core/Vixen.Rendering.Water | [35](plan/35-water.md)'s **W9**, the phase that turns the stack from built into reachable — and most of it is. **One mode where doc 31 needed two**, because placing a lake is placing an entity and editing its shape is editing a spline, and the editor already does both. **Draw is wired end to end**: `WaterMode` → `WaterModule` writes the `.vxspline` and creates the entity, ⚠ **clicking the first point again is how a closed body is finished** (the UI layer has no double click), and ⚠ **an unsaved scene refuses the draw** rather than making an entity whose curve has nowhere to live. **The zone panel is its derived numbers** — metres per texel, megabytes, vertices, height quantum, maximum amplitude — computed by the *kernel*, so it cannot be right about a configuration the renderer refuses. **And the wiring closes the island**: `AppGraphics` owns the `WaterZoneSystem` and the one `WaterClockSystem`, hands the zones to `!WaterSurface` on the terrain factory's terms and supplies them as `PostProcessVolumeSystem.Shapes` before the volume fold; `WaterPresenter` draws the same fold in an editor pane; the `overlay water` / `overlay watermesh` panels are doc 13's registry's first consumers; and the six `water.show*` verbs are registered explicitly by `WaterDebug.Register` and again as editor commands, because `ConsoleCommands.RegisterFrom(Assembly)` has no callers in the tree. **And the other two verbs are reachable now**, which is what W9 was owed. **Profile**: select the body, press `2`, and `WaterProfileHandles` draws three crosses per control point into the pane's overlay channel and hit-tests them in *render pixels* — a press grabs, a move slides the handle along the curve's own side vector and writes the component as it goes, and the release pushes one `WaterProfileCommand`. ⚠ **A press that is not on a handle is not taken**, because that is how the body being edited gets selected. **Preview**: `CarvePreview` was a flag nothing read because there was nothing to read it about — nothing in the tree ever *carved*. `water.carve` now regenerates the reserved `Water` layer from every body in the scene over every terrain in it, as one undo entry through `WaterCarveCommand` (which also resolves, without which a carve is deltas in a layer and a viewport drawing the old ground), and the toggle sets `TerrainEditLayer.IsVisible` and re-resolves. ⚠ **The terrain arrives through `ITerrainScene`**, the contract in `Core/Vixen.Rendering.Terrain`, not through `Vixen.Editor.Terrain`: the two are independent plugins and either may be absent. ⚠ **Leaving the mode puts the preview back on**, because `IsVisible` is saved and a view state that outlives its view is indistinguishable from data loss. *Owed*: `WaterBodyComponent` carries no per-body carve strength or bed layer — doc 35 § The body inspector asks for them — so every body carves at the draw settings' strength; adding them is a component layout change and therefore a scene-compatibility decision |
| **The water seam** — `Water/Surface.rvn` against the C# evaluator, on a device | ✅ | `Raven/Library/Water` · Platform/Vixen.Graphics.Golden.Tests | [35](plan/35-water.md)'s **§ D2**, written before there was a renderer to see it fail in, which is the point: `WaterSurfaceProbe.rvn` dispatches the library's `BufferedWaterWaves.Displace` over four thousand positions and times and the readback is compared against `WaterEvaluator` per component. ⚠ **The measured tolerance is 2 × 10⁻⁴ m where the doc asked for exact** — § Risks' own stated fallback, taken: the residue is a device contracting a multiply and an add into one FMA, it lands in the *phase*, and it is therefore linear in it (7 × 10⁻⁷ m under a hundred radians, 4.9 × 10⁻⁵ m at five thousand). Vixen's emitters do not contract; the Metal translation layer below the only Vulkan device in reach does, and no parenthesisation changes that. The structural half still buys most of the distance, since `OpSin`'s licensed 8192 ULP at those phases is a different wave rather than a rounding difference. ⚠ **The wave buffer is a `compose` slot and not a function parameter** — an unsized array has no type SPIR-V can express away from its binding (RVN4001) — so whoever holds the buffer holds the loop, which is the seat an FFT model takes when it lands |
| **Water's unblockers** — a volume shape interface, a third reserved terrain layer, a ping-pong pair, the scene-colour copy | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering · Core/Vixen.Terrain · Core/Vixen.Graphics.RenderGraph | [35](plan/35-water.md)'s **W0**, and every one of the four is written for a consumer outside water, which is the phase's whole condition. **B2**: `IPostProcessShape` replaces [32](plan/32-post-process-volumes.md)'s axis-aligned test with a box *and a sphere*, because an extension point whose only implementation is the special case ends up shaped like it; `Box` is the enum's zero so every volume authored before this loads unchanged, and `Custom` resolves through `PostProcessVolumeSystem.Shapes` — ⚠ **reaching nothing when nothing answers**, since falling back to the box would grade a rectangle around the lake while the inspector looked correct. **B4**: `TerrainLayerKind.Water` on [31 § D4](plan/31-terrain-grass-and-trees.md)'s contract with no change to it, plus `Terrain.ReservedLayer(kind)`. **B5**: `PingPongTextures` imports both halves every step and tracks the state each was left in — ⚠ **`Undefined` is not a neutral placeholder**, it licenses the driver to discard the previous contents, and `HasHistory` exists because the graph *cannot* catch a first-frame read. **B1**: `!Copy` is the resource, not the pass — the compositor could already say "read the scene colour, write the scene colour" and could not say the second target that makes it legal; it refuses a format or size mismatch by name rather than rescaling, and a copy nothing reads is culled with its target, so a document carrying a water node costs nothing without water |
| Grading LUT as an **asset** | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering.PostFx · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Assets | Both halves. The authoring one: `CubeLutImporter` reads Resolve/Baselight/Nuke/Photoshop `.cube` files, and **is now in `BuiltInImporters.Create()`** — it was not, so no `.cube` became an asset and the failure was silent (§ 1.6's row, task #167). ⚠ No mips and no block compression, deliberately — the reasoning is in `CubeLutImporter`'s remarks |
| Auto exposure — the log-average chain **and** a 64-bin histogram | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering.PostFx | Both of UE's meters. The histogram is **off by default** because it is a different question rather than a better chain — a geometric mean and a percentile disagree about a bright window in a dark room, and a document should not change meters by accident. See `AutoExposureRenderer.UseHistogram` |
| `AutoExposure.rvn` wiring | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Rendering.PostFx | `AutoExposureRenderer`: a chain of `ComputeRenderer` dispatches halving a 512-wide luminance image to 1×1, then easing the stored exposure toward it. `Tonemap.rvn`'s `UseExposureBuffer` permutation keeps the value off the bus and the change additive. ⚠ **The exposure buffer is imported into each frame, and into the *frame* rather than onto the compositor** — a re-declared buffer eases from zero and never converges, and a late import fails only the first frame. Both traps and the launch-seeding bug they hid are in [the PostFx README](../Core/Vixen.Rendering.PostFx/README.md) § Auto-exposure |
