Fully game-wired: node, factory, extraction system, atlas upload and mips, shadow casters and velocity, all spliced into a document by `TerrainFactory`. Proved by `Samples/03-PbrShowcase` — a committed multi-tile `.vxterrain` through a content build rather than editor machinery — and used by `Samples/13`. ⚠ **The placement rides the view matrix**, because `TerrainConstants` has no world transform: cull in the terrain's own space, or a terrain away from the origin culls the wrong nodes. Owed list in [the renderer's README](../Core/Vixen.Rendering.Terrain/README.md) |
| **Terrain collision** — the heightfield as a collider | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Terrain.Physics · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Terrain.Physics | [31 § B1, § D10](plan/31-terrain-grass-and-trees.md). **The runtime half is done and reached**: `TerrainColliderSystem` is one assembly with two references, opted into by a game — `Samples/13` builds it, so a character stands on the ground instead of falling through. ⚠ **Nothing in any project produced a heightfield shape until 2026-08-12, and falling through terrain is not an error anywhere.** ✅ **The editor half is fed (2026-08-21)**: `EditorApplication` contributes an `IPlaySystems` that builds a `PhysicsScene` over the world being edited on Play and disposes it on Stop, and `Editor/Vixen.Editor.Terrain.Physics` — referenced by nothing but its own tests until now — is a *module* in `EditorModules.Standard` that publishes the `ITerrainColliders` the toolset resolves and runs `TerrainColliderSystem` over that scene. `TerrainSessionTests.Playing_gives_the_ground_collision_and_a_stroke_rebuilds_it` asserts it against the editor the product starts. ⚠ **The blocker was a tier below the missing `PhysicsScene`**: until 2026-08-21 `PlayModeController.ShouldTick` had no caller outside its own tests, so a published `PhysicsScene` would have been a world nothing calls `Synchronize` on. That tier landed first; this row is the one above it. ⚠ **Physics belongs to play, not to editing** — nothing simulates while the editor edits, which is also what keeps the collider system's tile entities inside what a stop restores. ✅ **A project can declare its frame (2026-08-21)**: a system whose service the *editor* owns reaches a session through `IPlaySystems`, and a project's own is marked `[GameSystem]` and built by the session out of what those contributions provided. ✅ The discarded-`bool` hazard this row used to carry as open is answered by `TerrainColliders.Missed`/`Sync`. ✅ **The real latent bug was the tile index**, fixed 2026-08-20: `Rebuild(terrain, tileX, tileZ)` never validated it, and everything below indexes flat while `TerrainSamples` *clamps* — so `Rebuild(terrain, TilesX, 0)` silently overwrote tile `(0, 1)` with the edge row repeated at an off-grid corner, returned `true`, and stamped the aliased tile's revision so the poll never repaired it. It throws now |
| **Foliage and grass** — instance cells, the two-stage GPU cull, the scatter ring, impostors | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Foliage · Core/Vixen.Rendering.Terrain · `Raven/Library/Terrain` | [31](plan/31-terrain-grass-and-trees.md) T5–T7. Two paths that look alike and are not: foliage is *placed* instances persisted beside the scene as a `.vxfol` and culled per instance by `FoliageCull.rvn`, while grass is *scattered* by `GrassScatter.rvn` into a ring of device buffers as cells enter range and re-culled every frame. Both were built CPU-first on purpose, because a cull and a scatter fail silently in both directions, and the seam test compares the CPU reference against the shader as sets at exact tolerance. ⚠ **Owed, narrow and named in [the renderer's README](../Core/Vixen.Rendering.Terrain/README.md)**: only the ground casts a shadow, `ImpostorCapturePass` has no caller outside its tests, and a volume's palette is session state |
| **Texture streaming** — mip tails through the page service | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Engine.Renderer · Core/Vixen.Rendering | [22](plan/22-virtualized-geometry.md) improvement 6. `TexturePagePool`'s pages are fixed byte-size slices of a KTX2 file's level data, and it brought no budget of its own: the queue, the ceiling, the LRU order, the pinning and `Rejections` are `PageResidency`'s, unchanged — which is the claim `IPageStore` was making. ⚠ **Five page kinds now share it** — geometry, virtual shadows, textures, terrain tiles and foliage cells — and the last two are consumers doc 22 never anticipated. See [Streaming texture mip tails](guide/rendering/texture-streaming.md) |
| **Virtualized geometry** — cluster DAG, pages, hierarchical traversal, visibility buffer, material resolve, software raster | ✅ | `Vixen.Rendering.VirtualGeometry` · Core/Vixen.Rendering | [22](plan/22-virtualized-geometry.md) phases 0–6. Import → pages → residency → traversal → hardware raster → bin → shade runs on a device; phase 6's compute raster is the accelerator beside it, with clusters routed by projected screen size, the two rasters filling opposite ends of one visible list, and a 64-bit `atomicMax` resolving depth without an attachment. **Off unless a host sets a threshold** — the crossover is hardware's to say — gated on `HasInt64Atomics`, which forces the threshold to zero rather than branching anywhere else. ⚠ **The device half of phase 6's exit criterion has not run**: MoltenVK reports `shaderBufferInt64Atomics = false`, so the software-vs-hardware raster golden skips on the only Vulkan device in reach, and only the host half is asserted. Phase 7 (virtual shadow maps) is its own project |
| Deferred pipeline — GBuffer, shading-model dispatch, forward routing, decals | ✂️ | `Raven/Library/Pipeline` | Phase 10; cut-list #6. **The shader half exists and is gated**: `GBufferPass.rvn` (the same shader as `ForwardPlus` down to `surface.Compute`, diverging into three targets), `Deferred.rvn` (a fullscreen lighting pass with no `compose` slot, by design) and `GBuffer.rvn` holding the layout exactly once — all three in the library tree test, on both backends. The compositor already knows the stage shape: `RenderStage.ShaderComposes` and `PassAsset.ComposeFromMaterial` exist for it. **Nothing renders through it** — no node, no shading-model-ID dispatch, no automatic forward routing for non-representable materials, and no decals. ✂️ **Postponed past 1.0** (2026-08-19), on D3D12's precedent: forward+ meets the engine's needs and a deferred path is a second renderer to keep correct. The shaders stay gated, so the decision is reversible |
| Volumetric fog (froxel) | ✅ | Raven/Library/PostFx · Core/Vixen.Rendering.PostFx | [plan/06 § P2](plan/06-rendering-pipeline.md), which corrects its own "shares the clustering grid" premise: what is shared is the Z distribution and `ClusterGrid.Of`, not the grid. `VolumetricFog.rvn` is three compute passes behind one `Pass` permutation — inject the medium, light it, march a column front to back — each dispatch sized from what the *document* declared; `Fog.rvn`'s `Volumetric` permutation composites the result so the analytic falloff carries on beyond the volume's far plane rather than being deleted. The passes sit between `Lamps` and `Main`, the composite takes the `Air` seat after TAA, and the march is restated on the CPU against Beer–Lambert at four slice counts so the slicing neither adds nor loses energy. **All three items this row listed as owed have since landed** — `Shadowed` (sun in-scatter through the cascade atlas), `UseClusteredLights` (the per-froxel punctual loop) and `Reproject` (temporal). Remaining gap: the punctual in-scatter is **unshadowed**, because the punctual shadow atlas is a composed feature of the scene pass. ⚠ The far plane is the volume's own and never the camera's, and ⚠ in a local `PostProcessSettings` override `null` is "no opinion" while `0` is "no fog *here*" — both recorded in doc 06 § P2 |
| Contact shadows, light shafts, SSS blur, upscaler + FSR1 | ⬜ | — | Phase 10. Nothing in the tree names a contact shadow, a light shaft, an `IUpscaler` or FSR. Subsurface is the partial exception and is **not** this row: `SubsurfaceShading` and `Shading/Subsurface.rvn` ship the forward per-light half (wrapped diffuse, back-lit transmission) *and* the Burley profile a separable screen-space blur would sample — what is missing is the blur pass |
| Mesh shaders / meshlet culling behind capability flags | ⬜ | — | Phase 10; [22](plan/22-virtualized-geometry.md) is now the plan. `GraphicsDeviceFeatures.HasMeshShaders` is detected on Vulkan (`VK_EXT_mesh_shader` / `VK_NV_mesh_shader`) and refused elsewhere, but nothing outside the backends' own tests reads it, and `VirtualGeometry`'s `Meshlet*` types are the cluster DAG's rather than a mesh-shader stage |
| Golden-image fixture suite | ✅ | Platform/Vixen.Graphics.Golden.Tests | Six kinds — the backend at its simplest, one fixture per state bit a backend can silently ignore, the compositor, the UI, debug draw, and a whole frame per quality tier — plus device fixtures for clustered shading, GI, screen probes, surface cache, water, ray tracing and virtual geometry: 49 committed references across ~55 test classes. It caught two engine bugs **nothing but a picture could see** (a composed material parameter's qualified name depending on lowering order; one Raven struct used in both a uniform block and a storage buffer collapsing to one MSL type), and a third — an empty frame since the bindless-material merges — that **closed itself**, recorded rather than deleted because a defect that fixed itself can come back. ⚠ **The Linux leg is the only one that runs these**: macOS and Windows runners have no Vulkan driver and skip every fixture, so a tolerance impossible to meet cross-driver looks green to everyone but lavapipe. [The README](../Platform/Vixen.Graphics.Golden.Tests/README.md) carries the container recipe |
| `Samples/03-PbrShowcase` | ✅ | Samples/03-PbrShowcase | Converted to the standard frame: seven knobs stand where the raw-RHI passes were, the grid casts through the cascades, a baked Preetham sky is the image-based ambient, and the occlusion under each sphere is a real march over exact analytic distances. Also the terrain stack's proving ground — a multi-tile `.vxterrain` and a `.vxgrass` rule reaching the frame through the `extensions: afterOpaque:` splice, from a scene component and a content build. That run found the `.vxgrass` chunk unreachable at runtime, the terrain pipeline layouts placing set 2 at index 0, and the Raven fragment-stage `SV_Position` gap |
| **`Vixen.Video`** — managed WebM demuxer, codec seam, player with an audio-driven clock, YUV planes + conversion coefficients | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Video | 144 tests, and `Samples/11-VideoPlayback` drives it. Doc 06 § Other renderables. Landed far ahead of its Phase 10 slot |
| **`Vixen.Video.Codecs`** (Opus over loose WebM packets) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Video.Codecs | Split so a game with an uncompressed logo sting links no Concentus |
| **`Vixen.Video.Rendering`** — one pipeline, three plane bindings, `VideoRenderTarget` | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Video.Rendering | `VideoRenderer` converts the planes to an ordinary colour texture, and `Samples/11` uses it. ⚠ `VideoRenderTarget` and `VideoRenderFeature` — the "video into the UI" and ECS halves — have no caller outside this project's own tests |
| Video: MP4; a **material** (video lit on a mesh); frame-accurate seek; audio-track choice; subtitles; 10-bit / BT.2020 | ⬜ | — | MP4 is additive behind `IVideoStreamDecoder`, and `VideoImporter` claims-and-refuses it rather than half-reading it; the material is `MaterialRenderFeature`'s and Raven's. `VideoPlayer.Seek` exists but is the demuxer's keyframe seek, and `VideoPixelLayout` stops at 8 bits by construction |
| **`Vixen.Xr`** — session state machine, per-eye poses, asymmetric projections, runtime-owned swapchains, action input, ECS bridge, simulated headset | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Xr | All of it runs on a machine with no headset. ⚠ No sample or engine host drives it — the row below is why |
| **`Vixen.Xr.OpenXR`** — the three desktops and Android | ✅ | Platform/Vixen.Xr.OpenXR | The Vulkan instance/device/GPU are the *runtime's* choice, and the API shape makes that order impossible to get wrong |
| XR: a render feature; single-pass multiview; hand/eye tracking, passthrough, anchors | ⬜ | — | Two passes work today. Multiview's hook is `XrSwapchainDescription.ArrayLayers`, honoured by both backends; the `VK_KHR_multiview` half is `Vixen.Graphics`'s and is missing. Passthrough is the partial exception — `XrSessionOptions.PreferPassthrough` picks an alpha-blend or additive environment blend mode when the runtime offers one, and `OpenXrSession.IsPassthrough` reports it. Nothing names hand tracking, eye tracking or an anchor |
| Shader reflection: vertex input locations + push-constant stage coverage | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Shaders | A binding index is declaration order, so a literal in C# survives a renumbering and a generated constant does not — and the failure is silent CPU fallback, every frame |
## 1.10 Gameplay subsystems

| Feature | Status | Where | Blocked by / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Vixen.Physics` (Jolt 2.22.0) — bodies, shapes, constraints, character, queries, triggers, layers, CCD, ECS sync, debug draw, determinism gate | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Physics | Two binding bugs pinned by tests — [README](../Core/Vixen.Physics/README.md) § Two bugs in the binding |
| Physics on **iOS** | ⛔ | — | `JoltPhysics.Native` carries win/linux/osx/Android and **no iOS slice**; iOS is NativeAOT-only where a dylib would not load anyway. Needs a static `libjoltc.a` pinned the way MoltenVK is in `../build/native-dependencies.json`. Reason is recorded beside the `JoltPhysicsSharp` pin in `../Directory.Packages.props` |
| Per-pair collision suppression; vehicles, ragdolls, soft bodies; double precision | ⬜ | — | Out of Phase 8 scope. Layer-pair filtering exists (`PhysicsLayers`); `Foundation.Init(doublePrecision: false)` is the pin |
| `Vixen.Audio` — software mixer, buses, sends, sidechains, 14 effects, streaming, ECS, events, parameters, interactive music, capture, HRTF, loudness | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Audio | Far beyond the line the roadmap asked for; nothing structural is owed |
| `Vixen.Audio.Codecs` (Vorbis, Opus, ADPCM) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Audio.Codecs | Pure managed, rooted in the AOT probe |
| `Vixen.Audio.Physics` (Jolt occlusion), reverb zones | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Audio.Physics | Zones need no physics; occlusion does |
| Backends: OpenAL ✅, WebAudio ✅ | ✅ | Platform/Vixen.Audio.Backend.* | |
| Measured HRTF sets | ⬜ | — | `HrtfPanner` is a synthetic model and says so; measured impulse-response sets are content |
| `Vixen.Animation` — skeletal playback, 1D/2D blend trees, layers + masks, state machine, two-bone/look-at/foot IK, root motion, events, GPU skinning, key reduction | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Animation | Benchmarked; `ParallelThreshold` = 32 from measurement |
| A runtime path for `.vxanim` | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Assets | `AnimationClipImporter` compiles an authored clip to `AnimationClipContent` and a game loads one by address. `AnimationClipCache.Get` pairs it with a skeleton once per (clip, rig); `TrySample` reads a named target with no rig at all, which is what `Samples/13`'s box character uses. The sidecar's `extensions` block round-trips markup this build does not understand |
| Move sets — flat catalogue, scored query, bake-time overlay | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Animation | Doc [34](plan/34-move-sets-and-pose-constraints.md) P1. `MoveSet` is a flat array with a contiguous scan table; `QueryMoveSelector` filters on required facets, scores preferences and proximity, breaks ties on a stable `MoveKey` hash and retimes within the rate range. `MoveSetMotion` is a `Motion`, so layers, masks and events are unchanged. ⚠ **Owed: 5.9 µs for a 500-entry query with no required facet misses the plan's 5 µs by 18 %** (3.4 µs with one; zero-allocation either way, `MoveSelectionBenchmarks` records both) |
| Move transitions, phase sync and partial-body sets | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Animation | Doc [34](plan/34-move-sets-and-pose-constraints.md) P2. `ITransitionPolicy` fronts an ordered rule list, first match wins, and `RuleFor` says which rule decided a pair. `MoveSetMotion` crossfades with easing and an optional bone mask and carries the phase across — `SyncMode.ClosestFoot` aligns **contacts**, not fractions; `PhaseSource`/`IPhaseSource` lets a masked upper-body set hang off the layer below. Both exit criteria assert continuity of the *contact* clock over a walk↔run↔sprint ladder |
| Pose constraints — the stage, the four goal kinds, arbitration, and the frame's pre-evaluation pass | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Animation | Doc [34](plan/34-move-sets-and-pose-constraints.md) P3. `ConstraintStack` is an `IPoseProcessor`: position, orientation, aim and distance goals, each with a region and an **additive** form, resolved through `IConstraintFrame` against named `ConstraintBindings`, arbitrated by `IConstraintArbiter` and applied through `IChainSolver` onto `TwoBoneIk`. Residuals live on the *instance* — a clip's goal object is shared by every character playing it. D19's `IConstraintScheduler.PlanPreEvaluation`/`PlanPose` stage measures indistinguishable from a build without it. Traps and limits in [README](../Core/Vixen.Animation/README.md) § Traps. **Owed:** the arbiter has no skeleton-authored joint limits below the ones added later, and a chain longer than two bones is solved over its last two with the shortfall reported |
| Proxy shapes, surface coordinates and adapted sockets — one authored contact on any body | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Animation · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Assets | Doc [34](plan/34-move-sets-and-pose-constraints.md) P4, and its central claim demonstrated: one authored contact, three bodies of different proportions, the hand on the same spot on all three — asserted by projecting where the hand landed back onto the body. Seven primitives with a normalised parameterisation per kind, `ShapeGeometry.Project`/`Evaluate` exact inverses, `SurfaceFrame` as the sixth `IConstraintFrame`, `IProxyShapePoser` with lazy posing, and `AttachmentSocket` — a socket whose bone offset is itself solved, so one prop is gripped correctly by three hands. `.vxshapevocab`/`.vxproxyshapes` are real files whose importer fails naming the set and the missing shape. Measured: 8 shapes vs 120 on a body costs 902 vs 909 µs for 100 characters. ⚠ Ellipsoid parameterisation and basis order: [README](../Core/Vixen.Animation/README.md) § Traps |
| Trajectories — a goal that moves through a clip | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Animation | Doc [34](plan/34-move-sets-and-pose-constraints.md) P5. `TrajectoryFrame` wraps any other frame, so "this goal moves" is orthogonal to "this goal is on a socket". Two decimated polylines plus an optional surface path and rotation curve, RDP against an authored tolerance reported through `TrajectoryReport`. Exit met: a sliding-hand contact reproduces on a rail of twice the radius and nearly twice the length, checked at all 121 captured samples rather than at the surviving keys. ⚠ The surface path is what makes it portable; `U` unwrapping, the live frame resolve and per-goal phase are in [README](../Core/Vixen.Animation/README.md) § Traps |
| Root placement, camera framing and the LOD governor | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Animation · Core/Vixen.Engine | Doc [34](plan/34-move-sets-and-pose-constraints.md) P6. `RigidBodySolver` is one solve for the two bodies with no skeleton — a character's placement and a camera — so the camera inherits regions, additive goals, weights and priority from the pose arbiter. `root` goals decide where the character should stand, before the pose and excluded from it; the result is a suggestion the controller may refuse, as `LastRootMotion` is. `ScreenFrame` turns a framing constraint into an ordinary position goal. D22's three knobs are complete and `ConstraintGovernor` walks a bounded ladder reporting what it gave up — measured, a 150-of-400 budget left 35.6 % of the constraint time against 37.5 % predicted. ⚠ A satisfied region goal takes no share, and the governor reserves the floor for everyone still waiting: [README](../Core/Vixen.Animation/README.md) |

And **the same scorer really is the same object**: `UtilitySet` and `EnvironmentQuery` both implement `IScoredCandidateSet<T>`, one `IResponseCurve` *instance* scores an action and a point in the same test with `Assert.Same` on both sides, and `UtilityScoring.Combine` forwards to `CandidateScoring.Combine` — made true rather than asserted about, by moving the combining code and rewriting the streaming loop as a generic over a `ref struct` reader. `QueryEditorFactory` is registered in `StandardEditors`. 16 tests here, 6 over the world-facing half, 4 over the preview, 9 over the editor. ⚠ In doc 37 § P8: a test's purpose is Unreal's three and a filter is not a zero score; a `NaN` reading filters rather than scoring zero; test order is the file's and the runtime does not reorder it; a weight pulls a factor toward one rather than multiplying the score |

P9 finished it — `ITargetSensor`, `IGlobalWorldSensor` and `IGlobalTargetSensor` beside `IWorldSensor`, with a `SensorSet` running the globals **once a pass, cached**, so two agents standing together cannot see different weather. | VFX **GPU dispatch** (upload, readback, reaping, indirect draw) | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Rendering · Platform/Vixen.Vfx.Gpu.Tests | `VfxGpuSimulation` — storage, descriptors, the dispatch pair and both transfers — with the CPU/GPU agreement criterion asserted on a real device, validation-clean. **Reaping runs on the device**, as a third emitted kernel: every survivor claims the next slot with `atomicAdd` and copies itself there, so a reaping effect holds two full sets of the attribute buffers and the reap swaps which is live. `WriteDrawArguments` copies the counter's four bytes into a `DrawIndexedIndirect` command, so a draw reads its instance count from a buffer the host never sees. **Owed: nothing dispatches it, and that is four gaps rather than a missing call.** `VfxGpuSimulation` is constructed only by `Platform/Vixen.Vfx.Gpu.Tests`; `VfxExtractionSystem` contains no occurrence of "Gpu". (1) *Nothing supplies the module* — an emitted shader is per graph, `VfxImporter` "compiles and throws away" the Raven, `VfxEffectContent` carries no shader, and `Vixen.Rendering` links no compiler by design. (2) *Nothing can draw it* — the buffers are `Storage|CopySource|CopyDestination` with no `BufferUsage.Vertex`, and no `Raven/Library/Vfx` shader reads a particle from a buffer in a vertex stage. (3) *Spawning stays on the host*, so a device effect seeds through the `Upload` stall. (4) *Sub-emitters, `RecordDeaths`, `ParticleLights` and ribbons read a CPU `ParticleBuffer`*, so the selection must be an explicit opt-in with a stated refusal rather than a particle-count heuristic. ⚠ Not a gap: `WriteDrawArguments` leaves `firstInstance` a constant zero (asserted by `VfxReapTests`), so `HasDrawIndirectFirstInstance` does not apply here as it does to terrain's indirect draws. **Evidence that the backend agrees and that it ran**: `VfxBackendPictureTests` draws one graph through both backends with identical downstream code — worst channel 3/255, 25 pixels of 65 536 — and `Dispatches` on both classes is checked against `NullDevice`'s command stream by `VfxGpuDispatchTests`, because a constructed backend and a driven one are otherwise indistinguishable. ⚠ The survivors come out in an order the two backends do not share and neither promises — a particle's randomness follows its **identifier**, not its slot, which is why `VfxReapTests` compares the two as sets keyed by identifier. [README](../Core/Vixen.Vfx/README.md) |
| VFX **GPU sort** | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Rendering · Raven/Library/Vfx | Built and device-tested, **not blocked** — the roadmap's "blocked on Raven workgroup-shared memory" is stale: `ParticleSort.rvn` uses no shared memory at all. `VfxGpuSort` is a bitonic network, because a GPU comparison sort has to *be* a network — every invocation runs the same instructions, so a data-dependent partition is a serialisation. log²(n) passes with a barrier between each; 78 dispatches at the 4096 default, and `Passes` says so rather than making a caller count. **Owed, and it is the row above's gap rather than one of its own — not "simply not called".** `VfxGpuSort`'s constructor *takes a `VfxGpuSimulation`*, and nothing outside `Platform/Vixen.Vfx.Gpu.Tests` and `Core/Vixen.Rendering.Tests` constructs one of those either — so a caller cannot be added here without first closing all four of the dispatch gaps the row above lists. The CPU path sorts in `VfxGeometryBuilder` and is not an alternative that could be swapped out: its particles are in host memory and this reads a device buffer. Verified 2026-08-21. ⚠ The keys are sortable **integers** through the monotone flip, so the device order and `Array.Sort`'s are the *same* order and the test compares permutations position for position; the buffers are the capacity rounded to a power of two and the tail is **padded**, not the network truncated. [README](../Core/Vixen.Vfx/README.md) |
| Mesh/ribbon/light renderers, custom attributes, force-field/curl-noise/collision/sub-emitter/trail updaters | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Vfx · Core/Vixen.Rendering · Editor/Vixen.Editor.VfxGraph | **Most of this landed piecemeal and the row had not been re-read — twice now.** Built and tested: `VfxRendererKind.Mesh`/`Ribbon`/`Light`; custom attributes end to end (`ParticleBuffer` slots, `SetCustom`/`RandomCustom`/`CustomOverLife`, `VfxCustomAttributeRow` in the asset); `Attract` and `Vortex` force fields; `Turbulence` (the curl-noise field); `CollidePlane` and `CollideSphere`; `VfxSubEmitter` with burst-on-death, burst-on-birth and trail. ⚠ **Two of the four "owed" items this row listed were wrong, and a third was understated.** (1) *`ParticleLights.Collect` has a caller* — `VfxExtractionSystem.Extract` calls `CollectLights` after the step, `WorldRenderer` sets `Particles.Lights = Lighting.Lights`, and `ParticleRenderFeature.ReportLights` `Degrade`s a frame where nobody collected. Landed in `db4807a0`; the row was stale. (2) *`SetMesh` having no caller was the smaller half of the mesh gap.* Nothing could **describe** the draw either: `VertexSchema.Layout` resolved to one `VertexBufferLayout`, and no shader in `Raven/Library` declared the four instance inputs — so `Draw` bound an instance buffer at binding 1 that the pipeline had never heard of, and wiring the call alone would have thrown out of `VertexSchema.Layout` for an attribute the surface format has no data for. Closed with `VertexSchema.Instances` (a second buffer at `VertexStepMode.Instance`), `ParticleInstances.Schema`, `MeshParticleVertices.Schema`, `Raven/Library/Vfx/ParticleMesh.rvn`, `ParticleMeshMaterial`, `VfxEmitter.Mesh`, and `VfxExtractionSystem.Meshes`/`Residency`/`MeshMaterial`/`MeshVertexLayout` resolving through the *same* `GeometryResidency` the scene's meshes use. Proved by `ParticleMeshDeviceTests`, which draws two particles apart and counts the shapes — sabotage-checked by setting the step mode to `Vertex`, which merges them into one. (3) *The node library was missing four nodes and now has them*: `Vfx/Output/Mesh`, `Vfx/Output/Ribbon`, `Vfx/Update/Vortex`, `Vfx/Update/Collide Sphere`. (4) *The custom-attribute node needed a framework change first, and got one.* A node port is lanes of `float` and has nowhere to put an attribute's *name* — so `Vixen.Editor.NodeGraph` gained `[Setting]`, a **string-valued node field**: declared on the node type like a port, stored in the `GraphNode.Texts` table the graphics compositor has used since it shipped, edited through `NodeSettingMember` in the ordinary inspector, and written by a `SetPortTextCommand` that now takes a selection. Deliberately not a tenth `PortKind` — a kind that cannot be wired would put a socket on the canvas that refuses every wire, which is the argument `CompositorNode` already made. On top of it: `Vfx/Initialize/Set Custom`, `Vfx/Initialize/Random Custom`, `Vfx/Update/Custom over Life`, and `Vfx/Output/Ribbon` now **names** its strip attribute instead of numbering it — a slot is a position in the declaration list and that position moves when a block is added above. Three defects fell out of doing it: `NodeGraphClipboard.Copy`, `PasteCommand.Mint` and both of `SubGraphs`' node copies took `Values` and dropped `Texts`, so copying or extracting a compositor pass blanked every name on it; and a custom attribute's name becomes an identifier in the emitted shader, so `seed` or `age` compiled to source that does not parse — `VfxShaderEmitter.IsReserved` and `VfxCompiledGraph.Compile` refuse those by name now. **Still owed:** `VfxSubEmitter` has no asset form (`VfxEffectContent` carries no sub-emitters), no node and no runtime that steps it. A lit mesh particle is owed too: `ParticleMesh.rvn` is unlit by decision, and shading one belongs to the surface material path. [README](../Core/Vixen.Vfx/README.md) |

Neither library owns a scene query — `PvpMatch.Occupy` is told who is standing on a point. ⚠ **A matchmaking ticket is a *party*, never a player**, which is how "a party is never split" becomes a property of the types. ⚠ **`default(MatchPool)` admits nobody** — a positional record struct's parameter defaults belong to its constructor and not to `default`. TrueSkill's published reference figures come out at 29.396 ± 7.171. 80 tests; READMEs: [Instances](../Gameplay/Vixen.Gameplay.Instances/README.md) · [Pvp](../Gameplay/Vixen.Gameplay.Pvp/README.md) · [Matchmaking](../Live/Vixen.Live.Matchmaking/README.md) |
| **The world** — interaction and gathering, crafting, exploration, travel, vehicles with seats, leashing and spawning | ✅ | Gameplay/Vixen.Gameplay.Interaction · .Crafting · .Exploration · .Travel · .Movement · .Ai | [28](plan/28-gameplay-framework.md) **G7**, all six, and **none of them moves anything**. ⚠ **Movement's *transform* half is blocked on doc 16's owed parent-relative replication** — item 69 is still ⬜ — so what is built is the seat model. ⚠ **The AI half shrank twice**: threat and aggro went to Combat at G2 and the planners to `Core/Vixen.Ai`, leaving leashing and spawn tables. ⚠ **A shared node is *claimed* for the duration of a channel**, which is this section's duplication bug, and interruption consumes nothing. ⚠ **Crafting does not reference Interaction and `Travel` does not reference `Exploration`** — what each borrows is a *tag query*, which is what keeps the spine's edge list honest; travel's whole output is a `TransferOrder` and it does not take the fare. ⚠ **A leash needs two radii**, or a mob on the boundary flickers between chasing and resetting once a frame. 102 tests; a README per library under `Gameplay/` |
| **Owning** — plots, decoration, permission tiers, visitor access; collectibles, achievements, transmog, titles | ✅ | Gameplay/Vixen.Gameplay.Housing · .Collections | [28](plan/28-gameplay-framework.md) **G8**, both. ⚠ **Doc 28's "ten thousand houses are ten thousand rows, not ten thousand processes" is a claim about the API and is now enforceable by reading one: no method in either library takes a `now`** — no timer, no decay, no tick, and a plot's change signal is a `Revision` counter rather than an event, because a hibernating plot has nowhere to keep a subscription. ⚠ **A ban is a separate set that beats the permission ladder rather than its bottom rung**, or a house opened to the public admits the banned at the moment it matters most. ⚠ **G-Q3 was right about two things out of three**: a tag query is a standing test and cannot count, so an achievement has *both* `Requires` in the requirement algebra and `Criteria` on the kernel's event bus. ⚠ **A misspelt criterion verb cannot be caught by the build** — `Event.Kil` resolves to a real tag nothing ever posts; only a criterion with *no* verb is catchable. 72 tests; READMEs: [Housing](../Gameplay/Vixen.Gameplay.Housing/README.md) · [Collections](../Gameplay/Vixen.Gameplay.Collections/README.md) |
## 1.11 Editor

> **Phase 6's exit sentence is met.** The editor opens a project, imports assets, builds content,
> edits a scene, saves, and runs the game — entirely in `Vixen.Ui`, with no other toolkit anywhere in
> the dependency graph. The asset editors, the profiler, the debugger, the plugin host and the
> animation graph are all projects. What is left of that phase is **`PublishEditor`** with signing and
> notarisation, **golden screenshots** for editor layouts, and the **editor-shell performance bar**,
> which is unmeasured.
>
> ✅ **The viewport composes a real frame.** There are two presenters over the same three calls:
> `ScenePresenter` draws the editor's own instanced shapes lit by one key direction, and
> `FramePresenter` draws the scene through a real `GraphicsCompositor`. Both are reached; see the
> composed-viewport row below.

| Feature | Status | Where | Blocked by / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Vixen.Editor.Core` — `IEditorCommand`, `CommandStack` with merging/transactions/clean-marking, document model, `EditorProject`, `Selection<T>`, settings assets | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Core | 48 tests incl. randomised do/undo/redo. [Editor.Core README](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.Core/README.md) |
| **One editing pipeline** — `EditTarget`, `EditProperty`, `EditValue`, `IEditMember`, `IEditProvider`, `SetValuesCommand` | 🟡 | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Core | [36](plan/36-an-extensible-editor.md) § P1. The inspector is on it (`InspectorField` *is* an `EditProperty`) and so is the gizmo, through `IGizmoTarget.Record`. **`NodePortEditProvider` is the second provider** and the first outside the inspector: a graph node's ports are described as `InspectorMember`s, so `InspectorView` — not a hand-written panel — is what the shader and VFX graphs now draw them with, with multi-node editing, mixed state, a drawer per port type, a reset button and `<PropertyField Path="…">`. [Guide](guide/editor/node-port-editing.md). ⚠ **Terrain, foliage and blockout do not need a provider of their own, and this row used to say they did.** Terrain and foliage were already on the pipeline: every one of their settings objects is an ordinary `[Inspector]` C# class drawn by `InspectorView` through `InspectorEditProvider`, and `TerrainBrushInspector.vxml` is markup over one. Blockout's gap was a panel and it is closed: `BlockoutMode.Panel` names a **Blockout** panel of three `InspectorView`s over `BlockoutRetopologySettings`/`BlockoutChartSettings`/`BlockoutPackSettings`, the editable classes beside the `Core/Vixen.Geometry.*` records. ⚠ **Not an annotation on the records themselves, and this row used to say it could be** — they are `init`-only, they are `Core/`, and a record is replaced rather than edited, any one of which rules it out. ⚠ Still owed: **transforms** — ⚠ **and that row is blocked on a decision rather than on the work.** `EntityGizmoTarget.Record` is already one entry per gesture over N targets, so there is no coalescing and no multi-select to preserve; what stops it is that the seam cannot record a change that has *already been applied* — `IEditMember.CreateSetCommand` reads the before state itself, and `EditProperty` skips a write whose value is already held. Measured, both naive routes record nothing and one silently reverts the drag. See [36 § what routing the gizmo through the pipeline costs](plan/36-an-extensible-editor.md#what-routing-the-gizmo-through-the-pipeline-costs). Also owed: the on-canvas port fields in `NodeGraphView`, which still build their own editors beside the panel that no longer does |
| **Built-in features register like plugins** — `PluginHost.Activate(id, name, module)`, `PluginContext.FindMenu`/`AddSubmenu` | 🟡 | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Plugin · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Blockout · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Terrain · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Water · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Scripts · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Diagnostics | [36](plan/36-an-extensible-editor.md) § P3. `Editor/Vixen.Editor.Host` is the composition root; `Vixen.Editor.App` is a library taking its modules as a constructor argument, and **Blockout, Terrain, Water and Scripts are gone from its project file** — each registering its modes, menus and panels through the door a third party uses. Terrain needed `PluginContext.OnUpdate` and `EditorDocument.Saved`, which did not exist. ⚠ **`Assets`, `AssetEditors` and the diagnostics trio remain project references of `App`**, each for a measured reason — the import pipeline is not a feature, and the diagnostics *report* aggregates the project, the scene and the log ring as well as the profiler. Owed: moving `AssetEditorRegistry` somewhere both ends can see, which is what the last dereference waits on |
| **The contribution registry** — `EditorRegistry`, `NewAssetKind`, `CustomInspector`, `SceneTool`, `PluginContext.Owns`/`With` | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Core · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Plugin · Editor/Vixen.Editor.App | [36](plan/36-an-extensible-editor.md) § P2 and § D3. `OutOfTreePluginTests` compiles a plugin **at run time**, drops it in a folder, and an ordinary start-up loads it, its inspector element, its drawer and its tool. D3's attributes are built and reached: `DeclaredContributions` is an `IContributionScanner` registered by `EditorApplication`, so `[EditorMenu]`, `[CustomInspector]`, `[CustomDrawer]`, `[EditorTool]`, asset kinds, overlays, gizmos and icons mean the same thing in a packaged plugin and in a project's `Editor/` scripts. ⚠ **The scan is bounded and ADR-002 permits it only because of that** — one assembly just loaded or just compiled, never the installed set |
| `Vixen.Editor.Ui` — shell, docking, command registry, menus/toolbars/context/palette as views, theming, notifications, background tasks, localisation | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui | [Editor.Ui README](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui/README.md). ⚠ **Localisation is no longer here**: [46](plan/46-what-an-application-needs.md) § A3 moved `StringId`, `StringCatalog` and `Strings` to `Vixen.Ui`; what stays is `EditorStrings` (123 declarations) and `StringCatalogYaml` |
| Editor panels written in VXML rather than in C# | 🟡 | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui · Editor/Vixen.Editor.App · Editor/Vixen.Editor.AssetEditors · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Profiler · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Terrain | [36](plan/36-an-extensible-editor.md) § P4 ✅ — the path is walked end to end: `binding-path`, `<PropertyField>`, `MarkupInspector.Of<T>` over a `HotReloadHost`, and ~~nineteen~~ **forty-one** `.vxml` files across ~~six~~ **eight** assemblies — thirty-three panels and eight shared parts. ⚠ **That number is stale the moment a wave lands and this is the second time it has been found so**, so here is how to re-derive it rather than trust it: `find Editor -name '*.vxml' -not -path '*/bin/*'`, where the `-not` matters because `Vixen.Editor.Ui.Tests` copies this assembly's markup into its output as fixtures and a bare `find` counts each of them twice. [The panel ledger](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui/README.md#the-panel-ledger--what-is-markup-what-is-next-and-what-never-will-be) carries the per-panel breakdown and is the thing to edit. 🟡 because most of the editor is still hand-written C#; the ratio is what is owed, not the mechanism. ⚠ **The ratio has been surveyed once, panel by panel** — [the panel ledger](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.Ui/README.md#the-panel-ledger--what-is-markup-what-is-next-and-what-never-will-be) is what a wave should pick its work from, and it names the four shapes that decide whether a panel can be ported at all, the two recorded gaps that turned out to be already closed, and the five engine features that would unblock the rest. The denominator is **34 registered panels**, not the line count ⚠ **A fifth shape was added by wave 3 and the `FlameChartView` nomination withdrawn (2026-08-22)**: markup cannot write an intrinsic element's own `Text` — an interpolation makes a `text` child and an attribute on a lowercase tag is a selector attribute — so the escape is a *capitalised* tag, whose parameters are real property assignments and whose `ref`s survive a caller's `@for`. `Parts/FactRow.vxml` is the first shared part on that route, with four callers and a whole-tree rectangle dump holding it to the six hand-written copies it replaces ⚠ **`AudioMixerView` is ported and `FlameChartView` is withdrawn a second time (2026-08-23)**: the mixer was the named victim of both `change:` and `refs` and is now a `.vxml` whose rectangle dump is byte-identical in three states, and the escape from shape 5 turned out to be a four-line `UiElement` subclass rather than a part per caption. Two of the flame chart's three reasons died with it; the survivor is that `ElementState.Hover` is written in one place in the engine, reached only from a pointer dispatch, so an element a keyed `@for` creates between two pointer events cannot be hovered while the pool's never stops being ⚠ **`VariationHarnessView` is ported and the surviving-region rule generalised (2026-08-23)**: "a surviving key never re-runs its body" is a statement about regions, not about loops — `@if` opens one too, and `Switch` rebuilds an arm only when the *arm index* changes, so a binding that closed over an `is { } shown` pattern variable in a side panel keeps showing whatever was selected first. Unlike the `@for` version nothing diagnoses it: it is legal C# that is correct for the first value it sees, and the panel's whole existing suite passed because every test selected exactly one cell. The rule is that a binding may close over a region's *identity* and never over its content. The port also shows `refs` is not only for reaching a sibling control — a read-only grid needs one for its hit test ⚠ **The editor had never created a `HotReloadHost`** — the whole reload mechanism was built, tested and unreferenced until P4 |

Every property the generator was asked for is enforced without it: an id used nowhere fails `nuke CheckStrings`, an id repeated at a call site fails it too, a name that no declaration class declares is CS0117, and `StringDeclarationAnalyzer` (VXS0310–0312) refuses a declaration missing from `All`, two declarations under one id, and a `StringId` built outside the declaration class of an assembly that has one. ⚠ **Both declaration classes are covered, not just the editor's** — the analyzer travels with `Vixen.Ui.Generators`, which `Vixen.Ui.Controls` and `Vixen.Editor.Ui` both name. ⚠ **Owed and measured**: 178 ids are still built at editor-module call sites and declared in no class, so no translator's template carries them; `CheckStrings` logs the count |

| **Sub-object picking** — `SubObjectPicker`, `MeshElements`, `ISubObjectPicker`, `SceneViewport.PickSubObject` | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.SceneView | [24](plan/24-blockout-tools.md)'s B4: the ray test with a different payload rather than a new subsystem. A face is answered by a ray; a vertex and an edge by projecting positions, because neither has area. Innermost wins. ⚠ **A drawing vertex is not a vertex**: `MeshData` splits a corner per normal and per UV, so a cube's eight corners are twenty-four entries and its twelve edges are not in it at all — `MeshElements` welds by tolerance, not equality, because a sphere's seam is `cos 0` against `cos 2π`. Two limits are asserted rather than approximated: a face is a triangle until the kernel has n-gons, and nothing is occluded |
| **Snapping as one service** — `SnapContext` (element / base / modifiers), `ISceneProbe.TrySnap`, eleven commands and a Snap dropdown | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.SceneView · Editor/Vixen.Editor.App | [24](plan/24-blockout-tools.md)'s D4 and B5: an element set adding **edge** and **edge centre**, a **base** so `SnapBase.Pointer` puts the corner you grabbed on the target rather than the object's origin, three modifiers, and one context per editor shared by every gizmo and every `ScenePlacement`. The four old booleans are views over the element set rather than second state. ⚠ **`SnapToVertex`/`SnapToSurface` were honoured, tested and unreachable** because nothing turned them on — grep for who *sets* a flag ([SceneView README](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.SceneView/README.md) § Snapping) |
| **`Vixen.Geometry`** — `EditMesh`: faces over shared positions, an edge table that reports rather than refuses, face groups, corner layers | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Geometry | [24](plan/24-blockout-tools.md)'s D2 and P1–P7, all built: `MeshTopology`, `MeshSelection`, `MeshOperations` (fourteen verbs, ear clipping), `MeshShapes` (twelve parametric shapes incl. stairs, ramp, arch, pipe, door frame), `MeshSurfaces` (UV projection, smoothing groups), `MeshBoolean`, `MeshCollision`, `Stitch`. Under `Core/` referencing only the maths; the copies into `MeshData` and into the scene file live in the editor. ⚠ **Deliberately not a half-edge** — blockout geometry is non-manifold constantly, and a kernel that refuses that refuses the ordinary case; `MeshReport` says where instead. ⚠ **The boolean's classification is exact and that is the design**: a face's plane is three corners of the *original* operand, never recomputed. Every other trap — the two graphs, the relative area epsilon, faces renumbering, partial subdivision splitting neighbours, smoothing carried per verb, a shape sized in geometry rather than transform, `Stitch` after every cut — is in the [Vixen.Geometry README](../Core/Vixen.Geometry/README.md) |
| **An entity carries an editable mesh** — `SceneDocument.MeshOf`, a `mesh:` key in `.vxscene`, `EditMeshCommand` | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.SceneView · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Core | P1's exit: a cube in a scene is an `EditMesh`, it saves/reloads/re-saves to identical bytes, and moving one vertex is undoable. ⚠ **The record is flat lists with corner *counts* rather than start offsets**, so a bad merge is a short mesh rather than one whose last face reads off the end. ⚠ **Undo is two granularities in one type**: a position change merges across a drag, a topology change records the whole mesh because a boolean has no inverse. ⚠ **`parameters:` is written *instead* of the mesh** for a parametric shape — the geometry is a function of the numbers, so a file carrying both would carry two answers to one question. Materials per face group, smoothing groups and UVs joined the record with P5 |
| **Work plane** — `WorkPlane`, `SceneGrid` as a view of it, set-to-face / to-selection / to-world, offset along the normal, `]` and `[` | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.SceneView · Editor/Vixen.Editor.App | [24](plan/24-blockout-tools.md)'s D5. The floor grid is a plane you can put on a wall and build in, and every pane draws the editor's one plane. ⚠ **The adaptive 1-2-5 spacing stays and a chosen step overrides it**: a grid that only adapted could never be the four metres a level is blocked out at, and one that only obeyed would be a grey haze from two hundred metres up. `SnapContext.GridStep` reads the plane on demand, so the second number that could disagree does not exist |
| **Numeric entry during a drag** — `NumericEntry`, `TransformGizmo.Typed` | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.SceneView | Blender's `G X 5 ⏎`, which neither reference editor has. Every frame of a drag was already the pose at the grab plus a magnitude, so typing substitutes the magnitude. ⚠ **A typed number beats a snap**, being the most specific thing anybody has said about where the object lands. Axis letters override the grabbed handle, `Tab` moves between components, `-` toggles the sign, and backspacing the last character out puts the drag back on the pointer |
| **Dimensions during a drag, the tape measure, and scale references** | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.SceneView · Editor/Vixen.Editor.App | The readout is above the middle of the pane rather than in a corner, and shows the typed text, the drag's magnitude, or a measurement. `SceneMeasure` is two points a distance and three an angle, snapped through the same `SnapContext` a drag consults. ⚠ **`ReferenceVolumes` is drawn and not shipped** — lines in the pane rather than entities, so there is nothing to select, save, or leave in a level by accident |
| **`Vixen.Editor.Blockout`** — the blockout mode, its element modes, its selection verbs and its geometry verbs | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Blockout | [24](plan/24-blockout-tools.md)'s P0–P7: `BlockoutSelection` (loop, ring, grow, shrink, group, coplanar, linked, all, none, invert), `BlockoutGeometry` (fourteen verbs, one undo entry each), `BlockoutCreate`, `BlockoutCubeGrid`, `ShapeDrag`, `BlockoutSurfaces`, `BlockoutBoolean` and `BlockoutHandoff`. The mode implements `IViewportInput` as well as `IEditorMode`, so it takes hover, the `Ctrl`+drag extrude and the shape tool's two-stage drag, and leaves the rubber-band to the pane. ⚠ **Demotion of a parametric shape happens at the first edit, not on entering a mode**, so pressing `4` on a staircase costs nothing and extruding a tread costs everything; the badge is `IsPlainMesh`, derived. Every other trap — the `1`–`4` context split against view bookmarks, a boolean's operands as hidden children, the OBJ bake — is in the [Blockout README](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.Blockout/README.md) |
| **`Vixen.Editor.Terrain`** — the sculpt and foliage modes, the brush, the five panels, the layer and hole commands | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Terrain · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Terrain.Physics | Registered through `PluginContext` like a third-party plugin, with `TerrainModuleSession` binding the modes to whichever scene is open. `TerrainCursor` draws the brush on the heightfield it will land on, in both presenters; one drag is one undo entry; the panel is settings objects (`TerrainBrushInspector.vxml`) rather than dialog code. ✅ **A sculpt stroke rebuilds colliders through `Vixen.Editor.Terrain.Physics`** — `TerrainModule` takes `ITerrainColliders` from `PluginServices`, and since 2026-08-21 something publishes one: `TerrainPhysicsModule` is in `EditorModules.Standard`, and `Vixen.Editor.App` references `Vixen.Physics` and contributes the `PhysicsScene` its `PlayColliders` switch attaches to on Play. ⚠ **This row used to say no assembly under `Editor/` referenced `Vixen.Physics`, and that has been false since that date** — see §1.9's terrain-collision row for the whole chain. ⚠ **Play only**: a stroke made while nothing is simulating rebuilds nothing and increments `PlayColliders.Idle`, which is deliberate — physics belongs to play, not to editing. [Terrain README](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.Terrain/README.md) |
| Editor network panel | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.Debugger · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Diagnostics | `NetworkView.vxml` — Tools ▸ Network. The claim held: the panel is a *view*, and not one line of `Vixen.Net` was widened to build it. Three panes over what was already public — `BandwidthLedger`'s five breakdowns (component, field, remote call, object, connection) with its totals; round trip and jitter over the last 30 s, walked off a `NetworkSession`'s players exactly as `NetworkMetrics.Sample` walks them; and one packet through `SnapshotInspector`, decoded and applied to nothing. Five delegates from the host, pulled rather than pushed, because a panel factory re-runs on every reopen. ⚠ **The live-panel shape, written down because it is the first one**: the clock is `UiDocument.Ticked` throttled to 4 Hz, a reading is guarded by a four-field fingerprint against five dictionary sorts, and there is no revision counter — the summary is a record of *scalars* whose signal genuinely refuses an equal value, and the five tables are objects that hold signals so their `@for` keys survive and each row keeps its region on its own value. ⚠ **The graph is the one thing the panel *keeps* rather than reads**, because a filter and a running total are both "now": `NetworkTrend` is a 120-slot ring, in the editor and not beside the estimator — nothing else wants the history, and `NetworkMetrics`'s own remarks say rates are the collector's job. It is drawn as a ring rather than scrolled, which is worth 3 rebuilt regions per reading against 120, with the scale snapped to a 1–2–5 ladder to keep that true. **The graph has two loss lanes when the session's transport counts datagrams**, wired by nobody — a `NetworkSession` holds its transport and `ITransport.Loss` answers — and they are two lanes rather than one because *resent* is an upper bound on outbound loss while *lost inbound* is loss that happened. Each is a share of one interval's traffic and not of the lifetime totals, which is what a test asserts by making the link go clean and watching the lanes go to zero while the counters keep climbing. Sabotage-verified in `NetworkViewTests`, four ways — including one hole the first two rounds missed, where every test held its source still and a live panel never does. Owed: doc 16's ownership, interest sets and live RPC log, none of which the types answer. ⚠ **And owed harder than that row reads: nothing in the tree points the panel at anything.** `DiagnosticsModule.NetworkSession`, `.NetworkLedger`, `.NetworkRegistry` and `.NetworkSnapshot` are settable properties with **zero** assignments anywhere, tests included — verified 2026-08-21 — so every pane starts empty and stays there. That is not a wire somebody forgot: the editor runs no session, and the only process in the tree that holds a `DiagnosticsModule` is the editor. Feeding it needs a decision about which head owns the wiring — a networked play mode, doc 21's collaboration sessions, or the inspector protocol carrying a build's link stats back to an attached editor |
| Editor UI automation harness | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Ui.Testing · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Testing | Golden **screenshots** for editor layouts not started. `ViewportOverlayImageTests` and `ComposedPaneCaptureTests` are the pixel evidence that exists |
| `PublishEditor`, signing, notarisation, `.dmg`/AppImage/MSI | 🟡 | [build/Build.Publish.cs](../build/Build.Publish.cs) | `PublishEditor` publishes `Vixen.Editor.Host` self-contained and single-file per RID into `artifacts/publish/<rid>`, cleaning first, with optional Vulkan loader staging and an opt-in five-frame smoke test (`--publish-smoke`, opt-in because a published apphost backgrounds itself under a build script). Owed is the packaging half [12](plan/12-build-ci-and-testing.md) asks for — `.app`/`.dmg`, AppImage, MSI/zip — and the `Sign`/`Notarize` that follow it; nothing in `build/` calls `codesign` |
| `Vixen.Editor.NodeGraph` — model, generated registry, compiler, port typing | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.NodeGraph | [NodeGraph README](../Editor/Vixen.Editor.NodeGraph/README.md) |
| `NodeGraphView` (pan/zoom/marquee/wires/minimap/search-to-create) | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.NodeGraph | Over `NodeCanvas`. A one-way projection, rebuilt per structural change; drags write positions in place |
| Sub-graphs; undo commands; auto-layout; drag-from-port; preview layer | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.NodeGraph | Sub-graphs are **inlined**, not called. The layer draws a colour *or* a render target |
| **A shader-graph renderer that *fills* a preview thumbnail** | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.ShaderGraph · Editor/Vixen.Editor.App · Editor/Vixen.Editor.Host | **A sub-expression really is a whole shader**: `ShaderGraphPreview` copies the node and its upstream closure into a graph of its own, hangs a `Master/Unlit` on the output and hands it to the *ordinary* `ShaderGraphCompiler` — no second emitter, no second type checker, and the fixed vertex stage over a quad with identity transforms needs no special case. `ShaderGraphPreviewRenderer` compiles that text with `RavenEffectCompiler.FromSources` (added: the compiler took paths, and a temporary file per keystroke is not a compilation's business), draws it into a 64×64 `Rgba8UNorm` target and answers `INodePreviewSource`. ⚠ **Unlit into a non-sRGB target on purpose** — a preview shaded like the frame answers a different question in different units. ⚠ **Clip `y = +1` is the top**, so that corner takes `texcoord.y = 0`; `ShaderGraphPreviewDeviceTests` asserts a *corner* as well as a histogram, because a flipped thumbnail passes every histogram (sabotage-checked both ways: unwiring the master gives "1 distinct colour", flipping `v` gives "the top-left pixel's green is 253"). **Three invalidation gates**: the graph's revision, then the emitted text, then `RebuildsPerUpdate` — so a canvas redrawing at sixty hertz emits nothing and a drag compiles nothing. **The renderer owns one target per node**, redraws into it rather than replacing it, gives the image number up before destroying the texture, and `Created`/`Destroyed`/`Live` balance under test. ⚠ **The canvas's source is the *document***, which forwards — a session restore opens tabs before there is a device. *Owed*: a node needing a texture is refused rather than drawn (`Refusals`), because binding a material's maps is doc 08's. [Guide](guide/editor/shader-graph-previews.md) |
| Selectable wires; in-place sticky-note editing; inlined-node → source-node map | ⬜ | — | Nothing in the tree names any of the three. The last is what lets a diagnostic inside a sub-graph name a node the author can select |
| Raven-span → node diagnostics mapping | ⬜ | — | `NodeDiagnostic` exists and carries no span; needs the emitter to record spans as it writes |
| `Vixen.Editor.ShaderGraph` — node library, `DynamicVector` typing, Raven emission | ✅ | Editor/Vixen.Editor.ShaderGraph | Unlit, Sprite and PBR masters. Property names are authored and live on the graph |

Still nothing: operator nodes — the compiled form's parameters are constants, so an operator has to be constant folded — sub-emitters, and a live preview |
## 1.12 Networking

Phase 9 is the most complete phase in the repository — **all five exit criteria are met**.

| Feature | Status | Where | Blocked by / note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Vixen.Net` vocabulary, `ITransport`, `TransportConformance` suite | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | Time is a parameter, nothing delivered outside `Poll` |
| Transports: `Local` ✅, `NetworkSimulation` ✅, `Udp` ✅, `WebSocket` ✅, `Composite` ✅ | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net.Transport.* | UDP has cookie-based connect, fragmentation, all four channels |
| Transport: **`Relay`** | ⛔ | — | No code anywhere. Blocked on a scope decision, not on work ([roadmap](plan/14-roadmap.md) § Phase 9): a relay *client* with no relay *server* is untestable. Transport **fallback** belongs with it |
| UDP adaptive congestion control, ack piggybacking, path MTU, DTLS | ⬜ | — | `UdpTransportOptions` caps unacknowledged datagrams, which bounds memory but is not a window that responds to loss — the loss it would respond to is measured now, by `ITransport.Loss` |
| `Tick`, `TickManager` (drift correction), `PacketWriter`/`Reader` (never throws) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | |
| `NetworkSession` — handshake, clock, players, reconnect window, host/offline modes | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | |
| Bandwidth budgeting and priority shedding at the session layer | ⬜ | — | `BandwidthBudget` exists one layer down, in `ReplicationServer`; the session has none. The writer's overflow flag is what it would build on |
| RPC — generated senders, six pre-dispatch checks, ownership, rate limiting, manifest hash | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net + Generators | |
| Awaitable RPC (`CallAsync<T>` → `Task<T>`) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | Doc 16 said `ValueTask<T>` and was corrected |
| Broadcasts (`IBroadcast<TSelf>`) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | |
| `NetworkRules` policy (stricter-wins composition) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | |

| Replication — bit packing, `[Quantize]`, capture-once/copy-many, two-stage filter, ack'd baselines, shedding | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | |
| Field-level delta (`DeltaCodec`) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | 19.2 → 9.7 kbit/s a client on `Samples/08` |

| System that marks dirty modules once a frame | ⬜ | — | `NetworkBehaviour.MarkChanged()` is called by hand and no system calls it; wants the engine scheduler |
| Interest management — resolver chain, rules, `InterestGrid` (a source, not a filter), hysteresis, `NetworkLOD` rate | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net | Two corrections to doc 16 recorded. ⚠ Built and correct but **unreached** — `InterestChain` is constructed only by tests; no sample or game wires one |
| Team / room / fog-of-war rules; resolver **composition** | 🟡 | Core/Vixen.Net(.Engine) | Composition landed: `InterestChain` takes an `IInterestSource` plus ordered `IInterestRule`s, and `InterestGrid` + `SceneInterestRule` chain distance with scene. The named rules stay ⬜ deliberately — each is a game's idea |
| Motion — `SnapshotBuffer`, clamped extrapolation, `NetworkTransform`, owner smoothing | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net(.Engine) | 88 bits against 224 in memory |
| Per-axis enable, parent-relative replication | ⬜ | — | ⚠ Blocks [doc 28](plan/28-gameplay-framework.md) § Movement's *transform* half — a rider parented to a vehicle has no way to replicate |

A/B-asserted through a real in-memory build. Owed: a caller — no sample has a `.vxprefab` in a wired content build. 15 tests; [README](../Core/Vixen.Net.Engine.Content/README.md) |

What is missing is a message on the wire, and a caller for the `Destroy` actions |

| `SharpFuzz` with real instrumentation | ⬜ | — | Targets are already `(ReadOnlySpan<byte>) -> outcome`; out-of-process is also what would name a stack overflow |
| Generated encoders pinned end to end in the wire corpus | ⬜ | — | The corpus has `packet` (the primitives) and the generator's source is pinned; the composition is not |

| Metrics over OpenTelemetry (`NetworkMetrics` + `Vixen.Net.Telemetry`, OTLP push) | ✅ | Core/Vixen.Net.Telemetry · Live/Vixen.Live.Realm | Split so an offline game links no protobuf serializer. ⚠ **Was built but never fed until 2026-08-21**: fourteen instruments, a `Sample` whose remarks say "call it from the server's tick", and `Session`/`Transport`/`Ledger`/`Rpc` with no production caller — every gauge on every shard read zero, which is indistinguishable from a healthy link. `RealmHost` owns a `NetworkMetrics` now, attaches the session and the transport under it, and samples it once per `Update` beside `RealmHeartbeat.Observe` — which is what makes that type's "every one of these numbers is already an instrument" true. Replication, RPC and the ledger stay the game's to attach, because a realm does not own the game. A shard with an exporter passes `NetworkTelemetry.Start().Metrics` in through `RealmHostOptions` rather than getting a second meter under the same name |
